L607: ## The Applicant's Position:
L608: The clinical studies summarized in Table 21 provide the totality of data to support an NDA submission for nirogacestat in the treatment of adult patients with DT.
L609: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L610: Table 21 accurately describes the 6 clinical trials relevant to this NDA. However, the Applicant did not include Study NIR-OGT-201, which is investigating nirogacestat in patients with recurrent ovarian granulosa cell tumors; as of October 23, 2022, 1 patient has been dosed in this trial (source: Development Safety Update Report #12, reporting period October 24, 2021, through October 23, 2022).
L611: Not including patients who received nirogacestat at cross-over in NIR-DT-301, a total of 95 patients with DT received nirogacestat (n=69 in NIR-DT-301, n=9 in Study A8641014, and n=17 in Study 14-C-0007).
L612: ## 8 STATISTICAL AND CLINICAL EVALUATION
L613: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L614: Studies A8641014, 14-C-0007, and NIR-DT-301 are the relevant studies used to support efficacy.
L615: ## 8.1.1. Study NIR-DT-301
L616: ## 8.1.2. Study Design
L617: ## The Applicant's Description:
L618: Study NIR-DT-301 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, Phase 3 study to compare the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of nirogacestat and placebo in adult participants with RECIST v1.1 progressing DT. This study consisted of 2 phases: a DB phase and an optional open-label extension (OLE) phase. Nirogacestat was administered orally at a dose of 150 mg BID continuously, in 28-day cycles.
L619: In the DB phase, participants were randomized (stratified by target tumor(s) location [intra-abdominal or extra-abdominal]) to study treatment (nirogacestat 150 mg BID or placebo) in a 1:1 ratio. Participants were stratified based on target lesion locations at Baseline (intra-abdominal versus extra-abdominal) as participants with intra-abdominal tumors have been found to have a less favorable outcome as compared to extra-abdominal tumors (Howard &amp; Pollock 2016). If the participant had multiple target tumors located in both the
L620: intra- and extra-abdominal locations, the tumor was classified as intra-abdominal. Participants remained in the DB phase until death, disease progression, discontinuation from study treatment for any reason, or the estimated number of progression events were observed and the primary PFS analysis was completed. The primary efficacy endpoint was PFS, which was defined as the time from randomization until the date of assessment of progression or death by any cause (whichever occurs first). Progression was determined radiographically by independent, blinded Central Imaging Review using RECIST v1.1 on target tumors identified by the investigator at Screening or clinically as assessed by the investigator and confirmed by blinded independent central review.
L621: Participants were eligible to enter the optional OLE phase if they were enrolled in the DB phase when the primary PFS analysis was completed, or they met the criteria of PD as defined by RECIST v1.1 (confirmed by central radiological review). During the OLE phase, all participants received nirogacestat 150 mg BID and remained on study until death, radiographic PD per RECIST v1.1, clinical progression, premature discontinuation, end of study, qualification for Continued Access Plan, or nirogacestat becomes commercially available. The DB phase of the study has been completed; however, the OLE phase is ongoing.
L622: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L623: FDA agrees with the description of the trial design for Study NIR-DT-301. FDA adds that patients who experienced clinical progression were not eligible to enroll in the OLE phase of the trial, and these patients were not unblinded. Patients who enrolled into the OLE phase were unblinded. Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1) visit for the OLE phase was within 24 hours of the end of treatment (EOT) visit for the DB phase of the trial, and assessments and determination of causality of all ongoing AEs and SAEs that occurred during the DB phase were conducted during the EOT visit prior to unblinding the patient. A longer window between the EOT and C1D1 visit of OLE was allowed with medical monitor approval.
L624: ## Eligibility Criteria
L625: ## The Applicant's Description:
L626: Key inclusion criteria for the DB phase included:
L627: - Participants \ge 18 years of age with histologically confirmed DT (by local pathologist prior to informed consent) that progressed by \ge 20% as measured by RECIST v1.1 within 12 months of the screening visit scan.
- Were treatment naïve or recurrent or refractory to treatment with measurably progressing DT.
- Had ECOG performance status \le 2.
L628: - Had a DT tumor where continued PD did not result in immediate significant risk to the participant and had adequate organ and bone marrow function.
L629: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L630: The Applicant highlighted the key inclusion criteria. The following are additional important aspects of Study NIR-DT-301's eligibility criteria:
L631: - Eligibility for enrollment was not dependent on central review of tumor specimens. The patients were enrolled based on local diagnosis of DT, but tumor specimens were used to reconfirm the diagnosis of DT by central review for all patients. It is appropriate that the diagnosis of DT was reconfirmed by central review because it is a rare tumor for which expert diagnosis is important.
- Given that the clinical course of DT is heterogeneous, and active surveillance is recommended for patients with indolent and/or asymptomatic DT, it is appropriate for Study NIR-DT-301 to enroll patients with RECIST 1.1-defined progressing DT. FDA revised the indication statement consistent with this eligibility criterion.
- To be eligible for Study NIR-DT-301, patients who were receiving chronic nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs) as treatment for conditions other than DT must have been receiving them prior to the documentation of DT progression and have been on a stable dose for at least 28 days prior to the first dose of nirogacestat. Patients who were on any treatment (including NSAIDs) for DT must have completed this treatment at least 28 days (or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer) prior to the first dose of nirogacestat. It is unclear whether NSAIDs have anti-tumor efficacy against DT (the data is based on case series with very small sample sizes), but these eligibility criteria in Study NIR-DT-301 are appropriate given NSAIDs are used to treat DT, and the possibility that NSAIDs may have anti-DT effects has not been ruled out.
- Patients who had current or prior treatment with gamma secretase inhibitor or anti-Notch antibody, current or chronic history of hepatobiliary disease (except for Gilbert's syndrome, or gallstones), positive human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) test, positive hepatitis B surface antigen, or positive hepatitis C antibody or RNA, were excluded. Patients were also excluded with clinically significant heart disease or prolonged QTc interval.
L632: ## Study Efficacy Endpoints
L633: ## The Applicant's Description:
L634: ## Primary
L635: - The primary efficacy endpoint was PFS, which was defined as the time from randomization until the date of assessment of progression or death by any cause (whichever occurs first). Progression was determined radiographically by independent, blinded Central Imaging Review using RECIST v1.1 on target tumors identified by the investigator at Screening or clinically as assessed by the investigator and confirmed by blinded independent central review.
L636: ## Secondary
L637: - ORR, defined as a confirmed BOR of CR or PR by RECIST v1.1 during the DB phaseDuration of responses (in months), as supportive, descriptive analyses of ORR, defined as the duration in months from the time measurement criteria are met for CR or PR (whichever comes first) until the date of progression or death (whichever comes first).
- Change in PRO measures from baseline over time:GUnder/Desmoid Tumor Research Foundation Desmoid Symptom/Impact Scale (GODDESS)
- Brief Pain Inventory (BPI) short form
- European Organization for Research and Treatment of Cancer (EORTC) Quality of Life Questionnaire-Core 30 (QOQ-C30)
L638: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L639: Amendment 4 (dated July 7, 2020) of the protocol for Study NIR-DT-301 added clinical progression to the definition of PFS. During the review of this NDA, FDA inquired how the Applicant determined which patients experienced clinical progression among the patients who enrolled prior to implementation of this amendment. The Applicant responded that since the initiation of Study NIR-DT-301, the electronic data capture system collected "clinical progression" as a reason for the discontinuation of trial treatment, but there was not a protocol-specified criterion for "clinical progression." A specific criterion was added in Amendment 4, which defined events of clinical progression as follows:
L640: when the investigator determines the participant needs to be withdrawn from the study to start emergent treatment (i.e., radiotherapy, surgery, or systemic therapy including chemotherapy and/or tyrosine kinase inhibitors) for DT/AF due to the development of
L641: DT/AF related intractable pain, vital structure involvement, and/or progression of symptoms resulting in a global deterioration of health status
L642: However, Amendment 4 was submitted to FDA but not activated, and Amendment 5 (dated February 9, 2021) was where the addition of clinical progression to the definition of PFS was implemented. Clinical progression was defined as follows in Amendment 5:
L643: the onset or worsening of symptoms resulting in a global deterioration of health status causing the permanent discontinuation from study treatment and the initiation of emergent treatment (e.g., radiotherapy, surgery, or systemic therapy including chemotherapy or tyrosine kinase inhibitors) for DT/AF.
L644: FDA inquired who experienced clinical progression (regardless of confirmation of clinical progression by central review) prior to the implementation of the amendment. The Applicant responded that all 9 investigator-determined events of clinical progression (in this blinded trial) were reported prior to the implementation of Protocol Amendment 5 (dated February 9, 2021), and all were adjudicated by a blinded, independent, central clinical review (Source: SpringWorks's response to FDA Information Request in SDN 19, dated 3/23/2023).
L645: Although SpringWorks changed the definition of the PFS endpoint to allow clinical progression events, FDA communicated on multiple occasions (e.g., September 18, 2020, and January 22, 2021, meetings) concerns regarding the change in endpoint (also that inadvertent unblinding due to toxicity could result in bias in the assessment of clinical progression). Ultimately, the FDA review team remains concerned with the use of this endpoint for regulatory purposes. Although FDA acknowledges that patients may clinically progress, the lack of objective assessment (and noting the events are clinician adjudicated and not patient-derived [i.e., via a PRO]) may increase the risk of bias, precluding a definitive determination of a drug's effect in the clinical trial.
L646: These concerns were overcome; however, in this nirogacestat application via the totality of the following information:
L647: - Assessment of PFS results excluding clinical progression events (most events in Study NIR-DT-301 were based on objective radiographic findings)
- Assessment of ORR (tumor shrinkage) between arms
- Assessment of patient reported outcomes
L648: Finally, Study NIR-DT-301 did not evaluate OS as a primary efficacy endpoint. FDA did not object to this approach because DT is rarely fatal and the existing safety data for nirogacestat indicated that it would not be expected to lead to safety-related deaths. Because DT can be
L649: symptomatic however, treatment to induce delay progression, induce tumor shrinkage, and improve symptoms including pain can be beneficial and warranted.
L650: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L651: ## The Applicant's Description:
L652: The original SAP (v1.0) was approved on 07 April 2022 and was amended on 12 May 2022 (v2.0).
L653: The study sample size was based on the primary PFS endpoint. A total of 51 events was estimated to provide 90% power and a 1-sided type 1 error rate of 0.025 (1-side hypothesis) to detect a difference between nirogacestat and placebo, assuming the median PFS in the nirogacestat arm was 20 months and 8 months in the placebo arm (corresponding to a HR of 0.4 relative to placebo). Assuming a 10% dropout rate and a 20% spontaneous regression rate, 118 participants needed to be randomized in a 1:1 ratio to observe the estimated number of events.
L654: While the study planned to randomize approximately 118 participants, all eligible participants in Screening at the time of achieving the target enrollment (n = 118) were allowed to enroll, resulting in a total of 142 participants randomized. A relatively high number of participants were in Screening at the time of achieving the target enrollment rate of 118 participants due to the impact of COVID-19. Specifically, many institutions delayed study enrollment until summer 2020, resulting in a bolus of participants in Screening when the target enrollment rate was achieved.
L655: The intent-to-treat (ITT) population was the primary analysis population for the efficacy analyses. The ITT population consisted of all participants who were enrolled and randomized to study treatment (nirogacestat or placebo). Participants were analyzed according to the treatment they were randomized to and the strata to which they were assigned. Participants who were randomized but did not subsequently go on to receive study treatment were included in the ITT population. The Safety population was the analysis population for the safety analyses and consisted of all participants randomly assigned to study treatment who received at least 1 dose of study treatment. Participants were analyzed according to the treatment they actually received. The Per-Protocol (PP) population consisted of those participants who received study treatment and had no major protocol deviations; this analysis population was used for sensitivity analyses.
L656: The primary and secondary efficacy endpoints were tested in the following order: PFS, ORR, disease symptoms, impact, and quality of life evaluations by PRO. The primary efficacy analysis was performed after 49 events had been observed.
L657: If the null hypothesis was rejected at the specified significance level, the testing could proceed to the next endpoint, but if the null hypothesis was not rejected, all subsequent results were considered descriptive only. All data collected after crossover to nirogacestat in the OLE phase of the study will be analyzed and reported separately.
L658: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L659: The major changes in SAP V2 were summarized below:
L660: - Clarified that the formal statistical testing on PRO-related endpoints is conducted at the 1-sided, 0.025 level of significance.
- Supplementary analysis added where GODDESS Total Symptom Score is derived excluding item 2 (dull pain) and item 3 (shooting pain).
- Proportion of participants with improvement in BPI-SF API score at Cycle 10 was removed from the hierarchical testing of secondary endpoints and added as an exploratory endpoint.
L661: FDA acknowledges that the primary analysis for PFS was conducted at 49 PFS events, which was earlier than the pre-specified 51 PFS events due to the longer than expected follow up time. According to clinical study report, no new events were reported during Sep 20, 2021, and April 07, 2022, and the study was approaching 3 years since the first patient enrolled. The Applicant performed the primary analysis of PFS at 49 of 51 planned events. As the primary analysis of PFS was strongly statistically significant and clinically meaningful, FDA concurs with the reported PFS results.
L662: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L663: ## The Applicant's Description:
L664: The original global study protocol was finalized on 03 August 2018 and amended 5 times; the final protocol (Protocol Amendment 5) was dated 09 February 2021. None of the amendments impacted the integrity of quality of the study and/or its outcomes.
L665: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L666: FDA outlines the key protocol amendments below. All the amendments were activated except for Amendment 4. Some of the amendments were made to clarify PRO assessments and analyses. The change to include the clinical progression events could have impacted the outcome of the trial; however, this was addressed in the analyses and interpretation of the data below (and in the discussion of endpoints above). Additionally, although additional information was collected following Amendment 3 regarding ovarian toxicity, this information was not sufficient to fully characterize ovarian toxicity and resolution.
L667: ## Amendment 1 (dated November 27, 2018)
L668: ## 1. The Applicant revised PRO assessments:
L669: - To capture concepts important to patients with DT, the Applicant proposed to replace the MD Anderson Symptom Inventory (MDASI) with the EORTC QLQ-C30.
- To supplement the DTIS tool, the Applicant added Patient-Reported Outcomes Measurement Information System Physical Function (PROMIS PF) short form 10a and 3 items from the PROMIS item banks.
2. To describe the localization of disease more precisely, the Applicant changed randomization stratification factor by tumor location from “favorable versus (vs.) nonfavorable” to “intra-abdominal vs. extra-abdominal,” where intra-abdominal includes mesentery and pelvis.
L670: ## Amendment 2 (dated October 14, 2019)
L671: - Because DTs can spontaneously regress, the Applicant increased the sample size from 105 to 135 screened patients and from 94 to 118 randomized patients to account for a projected spontaneous regression rate of 20%.
- The Applicant clarified inclusion criterion #3 pertaining to DT (added bold font, and deleted font with strikethrough):
L672: Participant has:
L673: a. **Treatment naïve** Newly diagnosed, measurably progressing DT/AF that is **deemed** not amenable to surgical resection or radiation therapy **surgery without the risk of significant morbidity**;
L674: OR
L675: b. Recurrent, **measurably** progressing DT/AF following **at least one line of therapy** CR to initial therapy;
L676: OR
L677: c. **Preexisting Refractory**, **measurably** progressing DT/AF and has previously received therapy **following at least one line of therapy**, and the residual tumor has progressed.
L678: 3. The Applicant updated MSK/DTRF DTSS and DTIS to Gounder/Desmoid Tumor Research Tumor Foundation (DTRF) DESmoid Symptom/Impact Scale (GODDESS) to reflect the new name of the PRO
L679: ## Amendment 3 (dated January 27, 2020)
L680: - Because of reports of primary ovarian dysfunction, the Applicant included a requirement to ask women about their menstrual history and added monitoring of hormone levels in men and women in the Schedule of Activities. The hormones to be monitored were thyroid stimulating hormone (TSH), prolactin, anti-mullerian hormone (AMH), luteinizing hormone
L681: (LH), follicle stimulating hormone (FSH), estradiol, and progesterone for women; and total testosterone, free testosterone, progesterone, FSH, and LH for men.
L682: Amendment 4 (dated July 7, 2020) – not activated
L683: - The Applicant added clinical progression into the definition of PFS definition as described in “Study Efficacy Endpoints” in Section 8.1.2 of this NDA review.
- To minimize the potential for bias in the assessment of clinical progression, the Applicant added that patients who discontinue due to clinical progression are not eligible for participation in the optional OLE phase.
- The Applicant added that patients who discontinue treatment due to clinical progression will be unblinded at the EOT visit for the DB phase to help inform future treatment decisions.
- The Applicant added that patients who discontinue due to reasons other than disease progression will not be unblinded and will not be eligible for participation in the optional OLE phase of the trial.
L684: Amendment 5 (February 9, 2021)
L685: - The Applicant revised the definition of clinical progression as described in “Study Efficacy Endpoints” in Section 8.1.2 of this NDA review. See comments above in FDA discussion of endpoints regarding the addition of clinical progression events.
- The Applicant clarified that only patients who discontinue due to radiographic progression will be eligible for unblinding and participation in the OLE.
- To minimize the potential for bias in the assessment of clinical progression, the Applicant changed the protocol to state that patients who discontinue treatment due to clinical progression will NOT be unblinded.
L686: ## 8.1.3. Study Results
L687: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L688: ## Data
L689: This study was conducted in accordance with the protocol and the consensus ethical principles derived from international guidelines including the Declaration of Helsinki and Council for International Organizations of Medical Sciences International Ethical Guidelines, applicable ICH GCP guidelines, and all applicable local laws and regulations.
L690: ## The Applicant's Position:
L691: Study NIR-DT-301 is GCP compliant.
L692: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L693: Section 10.1.1 of the clinical protocol for Study NIR-DT-301 states the trial was conducted in accordance with the applicable International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) Good Clinical Practice (GCP) Guidelines, and applicable laws and regulations.
L694: ## Financial Disclosure
L695: ## The Applicant's Position:
L696: All investigators have been researched for conflicts of interest. None were identified as having financial interests/arrangements with the sponsor.
L697: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L698: The Applicant submitted a Financial Interests and Arrangements of Clinical Investigators Certification, which states that the Applicant did not enter into any financial arrangements with the clinical investigators. However, FDA inspection revealed that four investigators had financial arrangements, none of which were disclosed by the Applicant. See Section 4.1 for more details.
L699: ## Patient Disposition
L700: ## Data
L701: The data cutoff and database freeze dates for this CSR were 07 April 2022 and 17 May 2022, respectively. Only assessments conducted through 07 April 2022 were analyzed for the primary analysis.
L702: A summary of the disposition of all participants enrolled is presented in Table 22. A total of 142 participants were randomized, with 141 participants receiving study treatment (nirogacestat or placebo) in the DB phase. The majority of randomized participants had extra-abdominal (109 participants [77%]) versus intra-abdominal (33 participants [23%]) tumors.
L703: As of the data cutoff, 59 participants were ongoing in the DB phase and 47 participants discontinued study treatment due to progression events meeting the study endpoint. In the table below, progression events meeting the study endpoint reflected the total number of events resulting in the discontinuation of study treatment, not in the analysis of PFS. In the placebo arm, 1 participant discontinued study treatment due to an AE prior to death, which counted as an event in the PFS analysis. Another placebo participant discontinued due to other
L704: reasons prior to receiving central imaging results confirming PD and was also counted as an event in the PFS analysis.
L705: A total of 35 participants prematurely discontinued DB treatment. The most common reasons for premature discontinuation were “Other” (16 participants) and “AE” (15 participants).
L706: At the time of the data cut-off date, 32 of 40 eligible participants enrolled in the OLE phase of the study. The 59 participants that were still ongoing in the DB phase were not included in the count of participants eligible for the OLE phase.

L720: ## The Applicant's Position:
L721: Participants by randomized strata were balanced between treatment arms. A higher proportion of participants in the nirogacestat arm than in the placebo arm were ongoing at the time of the data cut, largely due to a lower proportion of participants with events meeting the study endpoint in the nirogacestat arm compared with the placebo arm, including participants with radiographic PD.
L722: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L723: FDA agrees with the data presented in Table 22. Most of the ITT population received at least 1 dose of trial treatment (99% vs. 100% in nirogacestat, and placebo arms, respectively). FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that the randomized strata (intra-abdominal vs. extra-abdominal tumor) were well-balanced between the arms, which is important given that
L724: patients with intra-abdominal tumors have a worse prognosis. Patients in the nirogacestat arm were less likely to have met the PFS endpoint (17% vs. 49% for nirogacestat, and placebo arms, respectively), and more likely to be receiving ongoing treatment at data cutoff date (51% vs. 32% for nirogacestat, and placebo arms, respectively). Only one patient had a qualified clinical progression in the nirogacestat arm, but the central review of this patient's tumor specimen determined that the tumor was not a DT, and this patient was not included in the per-protocol population (PP). Thus, focusing only on the PP population (n=62 in nirogacestat, n=67 in placebo), patients in the nirogacestat arm remain less likely to have met the PFS endpoint, and more likely to be on ongoing treatment at data cutoff date (Table 23).

L740: Most patients in the placebo arm who were eligible to enroll in the OLE phase did enroll (97%, n=28/29), whereas a smaller proportion of the eligible patients in the nirogacestat arm enrolled into the OLE phase (36%, n=4/11), suggesting that patients who had radiographic progression while on nirogacestat preferred to discontinue treatment.
L741: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L742: ## Data:
L743: A total of 38 participants (54%) in the nirogacestat arm and 47 participants (65%) in the placebo arm had at least 1 CSR-reportable protocol deviation. At least 1 major CSR-reportable protocol deviation occurred in 4 participants (6%) in the nirogacestat arm and 4 participants (6%) in the placebo arm. These major CSR-reportable protocol deviations included mis-stratification based on incorrect tumor location and enrollment without meeting inclusion/exclusion criteria.
L744: ## The Applicant's Position:
L745: A CSR-reportable protocol deviation is considered a significant deviation that may impact the accuracy, and/or reliability of the study data or participants rights, safety, or well-being. A major CSR-reportable protocol deviation would exclude the participant from the PP population and include participants that did not meet inclusion/exclusion criteria,
L746: mis-randomizations, mis-stratifications, or may impact accuracy and reliability of the study data.
L747: The 8 participants with major CSR-reportable protocol deviations excluded from the PP population were not expected to impact the data integrity for the analysis, which was confirmed by the sensitivity analysis of PFS comparing the ITT population (HR = 0.29 [95% CI: 0.15, 0.55]; p &lt; 0.001) versus the PP population (HR = 0.26 [95% CI: 0.13, 0.53]; p &lt; 0.001).
L748: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L749: Table 25 outlines the protocol deviations in Study NIR-DT-301, including the details regarding the major deviations. Based on the protocol deviations dataset (ADDV), most patients had protocol deviations, and 8 patients (6%) had major protocol deviations.
L750: In addition, FDA noted that five patients underwent concomitant excisions of DTs and one patient underwent concomitant radiotherapy of a bleeding DT (see "Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use" in Section 8.1.2); 3 patients were in the nirogacestat arm, and 3 patients were in the placebo arm. These events were not reported as protocol deviations, and the protocol for Study NIR-DT-301 states that potentially curative radiotherapy, and surgical resection of DT were prohibited concomitant procedures. It is possible that these procedures were not reported as protocol deviations because the excisions were not curative, i.e., the patients likely had other remaining DT lesions that were not excised, and the radiotherapy was not considered therapeutic. However, these procedures still have the potential to effect efficacy outcomes, particularly imaging-based outcomes such as PFS and ORR.
L751: For FDA's sensitivity analysis of PFS comparing the ITT population to the population of patients who did not have major protocol deviations, FDA excluded the five patients who had excisions of DT, and the one patient who underwent radiotherapy from the PP population (i.e., revised PP population). The PFS HR was 0.25 [95% CI: (0.12, 0.52)] with median PFS was not reached for the nirogacestat and 15.4 months for the placebo arm in the revised PP population. FDA agrees with the Applicant that the presence of these protocol deviations did not impact the interpretation of the PFS results.

L757: One patient was reported to be “prematurely” unblinded (patient ID (b) (6)); the patient was on the placebo arm and had a qualified clinical progression. Based on the CRF and narrative, this event was not considered a protocol deviation because the unblinding followed pre-specified unblinding rules, and this was confirmed by the Applicant. The patient was unblinded 22 months after the patient was discontinued from trial treatment. The blind was broken because the patient wanted to start a new therapy and needed to know if they were on the nirogacestat arm or the placebo arm prior to starting new therapy. FDA agrees that this premature unblinding was not a protocol deviation. FDA’s OSI did not observe issues related to accidental unblinding during the inspection of the three clinical sites and the Applicant.

L770: ## The Applicant's Position:
L771: Overall, baseline characteristics were similar between treatment groups. The study randomized 142 participants, of which 65% were female, which is consistent with the overall DT population that is more commonly reported in females (Anneberg et al. 2022; Skubitz 2017; de Camargo et al. 2010; Reitamo et al. 1982). Given the eligibility requirement for ≥ 20% disease progression per RECIST v1.1, the study selected a participant population with more severe disease than previously reported in the literature. This is evidenced by the high rate of multi-focal disease (40%) as compared to prior studies (~10%) (Peng et al. 2012), relatively large median Baseline target tumor(s) size (92 mm), and high rate of prior treatment (74%) including
L772: a median of at least 2 prior lines of therapy with the majority of participants being considered refractory or recurrent following prior treatments.
L773: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L774: The trial population is adequately representative of the general population of patients with DT in that the ratio of female to male is approximately 2:1, and the average age of the patients was in the mid-30s, which is the age of peak incidence for DT. The trial population's baseline characteristics were generally well-balanced across the arms, except for the numerical differences in race and ethnicity. DT does not have a known predilection for a certain race or ethnicity, so the slight imbalance in the distribution of race and ethnicity in Study NIR-DT-301 does not impact the integrity of the trial. However, because DT is not known to have a predilection for a certain races or ethnicities, it may have been feasible to enroll more racial and ethnic minorities into this trial to better represent the U.S. population, particularly when most of the patients were enrolled in the U.S. (n=90, 63%). For example, in a cohort study of patients with DT across three academic centers in the U.S. (over a 28-year period), 69% were White, 23% were Black, and 9% were reported as "Other race" (Peng et al., 2012).
L775: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L776: ## Data
L777: A summary of disease characteristics in the ITT population is presented in Table 27. A higher rate of multifocal disease was observed in this study as compared with prior studies in DT (40% vs ~10% observed in prior studies with DT) (Peng et al. 2012). The majority of participants had received prior treatment for DT and were classified as refractory or recurrent, 69% and 8%, respectively; the treatment arms were well-balanced.
L778: Genetic results were determined per central review of tumor tissue (somatic) and blood (germline) mutations. The majority (81%) of participants who were evaluable for somatic mutations had somatic mutations in *CTNNB1*. A slightly higher percentage of evaluable participants reported mutations in *APC* (21% versus ~10%) than typically reported in the literature (Riedel &amp; Agulnik 2022; Kasper et al. 2011). A total of 15 participants reported germline mutations of *APC*, which were consistent with their tumors. Twenty-four participants had a family history of FAP including 12 with confirmed *APC* mutations (Table 27). The remaining 12 participants with FAP did not have germline mutations identified (n = 2) or did not have samples available for germline analysis (n = 10).
L779: The median Baseline target tumor size per RECIST was 100.35 mm, with a range from 19.9 to 400.9 mm with a slightly larger target tumor size of 115.7 mm in the placebo arm as compared to the nirogacestat arm 91.6 mm.

L790: ## The Applicant's Position:
L791: Disease characteristics were generally balanced between treatment arms and consistent with the disease under study.
L792: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L793: There were small numerical differences regarding tumor characteristics across the treatment arms (Table 28, and Table 29). Slightly more patients in the placebo arm had protocol-defined refractory disease, larger number and size of target lesions based on RECIST 1.1, and more prior therapy (specifically, NSAIDs, tamoxifen, and surgery). However, slightly more patients in the nirogacestat arm had recurrent disease and larger tumor volumes based on MRI. Patients in the placebo arm had shorter time between diagnosis and first treatment, including first systemic treatment, but patients in the nirogacestat arm had shorter time between last treatment and randomization. Patients in both arms had similar time between diagnosis and randomization. Thus, the disease severity across the two arms appears generally balanced across the two arms.

L800: The genetic characteristics were similar across the treatment arms (Table 29). Most patients (n=110, 77%) in the ITT population had a mutation in CTNNB1 (n=85, 60%) or APC (n=29, 20%). Two additional patients had a somatic mutation in a different gene (data not presented in dataset). FDA agrees with the Applicant in that the proportion of patients with the APC mutation is higher compared to the general population (approximately 10%) although we were not able to confirm the specific numbers described in "data" above (FDA's analysis is shown in the table below). All detected CTNNB1 mutations were somatic, and 76% (n=22) of all detected APC mutations were somatic. The proportion of patients with germline APC (n=21, 15%) in this trial is slightly higher than the proportion in the general DT population (approximately 5-10%) (de Camargo et al., 2010; Skubitz et al., 2017). The majority, but not all, of the patients with
L801: germline APC mutations had an APC mutation in the DT (Table 29). Similarly, the majority, but not all, of the patients with family history of FAP had germline APC mutations (Table 29).
L802: Four patients (2.8%, n=3 nirogacestat, n=1 placebo) had mutations in both CTNNB1 and APC genes, and mutations were all somatic in all 4 patients, which is expected since somatic mutations of CTNNB1 and germline mutations of APC are probably mutually exclusive (Penel et al., 2017).

L806: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L807: ## Data
L808: Study treatment exposure is presented in Table 30. Median duration of study treatment exposure was 20.57 months in the nirogacestat arm, which was nearly double that observed in the placebo arm (11.40 months) likely due to the greater number of events observed in the placebo arm. The majority of participants receiving nirogacestat (65%) remained on study treatment for more than 13 cycles, suggesting sustained tolerability over an extended period of treatment. The total duration of exposure was 99.18 participant-years for the nirogacestat arm and 79.42 participant-years for the placebo arm.
L809: A total of 139 participants (99%) received any concomitant medication, including 69 participants (100%) in the nirogacestat arm and 70 participants (97%) in the placebo arm. The most frequently reported (\ge 30% overall) Anatomical Therapeutic Chemical (ATC) classifications were anilides (67 participants [48%]), other viral vaccines (50 participants [35%]), serotonin antagonists (48 participants [34%]), antipropulsives (47 participants [33%]), natural opium alkaloids (47 participants [33%]), and propionic acid derivatives (44 participants [31%]).
L810: A total of 55 participants (39%) had any concomitant procedures, including 33 participants (48%) in the nirogacestat arm and 22 participants (31%) in the placebo arm. Overall, concomitant procedures by system organ class (SOC) were surgical and medical procedures (42 participants [30%]) and investigations (33 participants [23%]).

L816: a Duration of exposure was calculated as: (date of last dose - date of first dose + 1)/ 30.4375.
L817: b Actual dose intensity was calculated as: cumulative dose received/duration of the exposure in days. If a participant's most recent dose modification was an interruption and the participant had not discontinued, days of interruption up until the data cut point were added to the denominator.
L818: c Relative dose intensity was calculated as: 100 \times (\text{cumulative dose received}/\text{planned dose received}). Planned dose was 300 mg/day \times duration of exposure in days. If a participant's most recent dose modification was an interruption and the participant had not discontinued, days of interruption up until the data cut point were added to the denominator.
L819: d Dose modifications included both reductions and interruptions.
L820: ## The Applicant's Position:
L821: The median duration of study treatment exposure in the nirogacestat arm was nearly twice that of the placebo arm, likely due to the greater number of events observed in the placebo arm. Consistent with the protocol, which mandated a dose reduction to 100 mg BID for the management of select, persistent Grade \ge 3 AEs (diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, skin toxicity, hypophosphatemia, hematologic/nonhematologic toxicities) and was optional for Grade 2 OD, 42% of participants receiving nirogacestat had dose reductions. The median duration of study drug exposure after reduction was 19.06 months. The majority of participants receiving nirogacestat (65%) remained on study treatment for more than 13 cycles, suggesting sustained tolerability over an extended period of treatment.
L822: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L823: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that patients in the nirogacestat arm had longer treatment exposure than the patients in the placebo arm, likely due to fewer progression events in the nirogacestat arm. Patients in the nirogacestat arm had lower dose intensity (both actual and relative), likely due to dose reductions and interruptions being more frequent in the nirogacestat arm. Most patients in the nirogacestat arm had dose modifications (65%), with dose interruptions (65%) being more common than dose reductions (42%). TEAEs were the most common reasons for dose modifications in both treatment arms.
L824: FDA conducted additional analysis to investigate the time to first dose modification and total time of dose interruption (Table 31). Patients in the nirogacestat arm interrupted dosing earlier than patients in the placebo arm (mean 4.5 cycles vs. 6.3 cycles, respectively). Patients in the nirogacestat arm had their first dose reduction at approximately the same time as their first dose interruption, i.e., mean of 4.9 cycles; no patients in the placebo arm had a dose reduction. The total duration of dose interruption was similar across the two arms (mean 22.3 vs. 26.7 days for nirogacestat vs. placebo arm, respectively). Note, FDA sent an Information Request to the Applicant to clarify why the sample size for "time to first dose interruption" and "total time interrupted" were different for the nirogacestat arm (see Table 31). The Applicant responded that one patient who had a dose interruption due to an adverse event (ID [REDACTED](b)(6) in the nirogacestat arm) discontinued treatment, so this patient was not counted in the "total time interrupted" because the patient did not restart nirogacestat.
L825: In conclusion, even though the patients in the nirogacestat arm tolerated long exposure to nirogacestat, the earlier and more frequent dose modifications suggest that many patients were not able to tolerate nirogacestat 150 mg PO BID.

L828: Source: ADEX (SDN 39; May 5, 2023)
L829: Most patients in Study NIR-DT-301 received concomitant medications (100% and 97% of the safety population in the nirogacestat and placebo arms respectively). FDA analyzed the common indications for concomitant medications (see table below). The most common indication for concomitant medications was pain, and tumor pain occurred more frequently in the placebo arm (46%) than the nirogacestat arm (33%). Diarrhea, nausea, rash or dermatitis, hypophosphatemia, and headache occurred more frequently in the nirogacestat arm, likely due to the adverse reactions associated with nirogacestat. Patients in the nirogacestat arm were also more likely to have received concomitant medications for infections, both COVID-19 and non-COVID-19 related, than patients in the placebo arm. FDA analysis of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) also demonstrated that patients in the nirogacestat arm had more TEAEs in the System, Organ, Class (SOC) of Infections and Infestations than patients in the placebo arm, 57% (n=39) vs. 33% (n=24), respectively. However, the rate of serious TEAEs in the SOC of Infections and Infestations were similar across the two arms: 6% (n=4) in nirogacestat and 7% (n=5) in placebo arms. The serious infections in the nirogacestat arm were appendicitis, infected cyst, abdominal infection and abscess, and groin abscess (n=1 for each serious infection). The serious infections in the placebo arm were COVID-19 (n=2), sepsis (n=2), and
L830: infection, and sepsis (n=1).
L831: Analysis of the common indications for use of concomitant medications suggest that nirogacestat has anti-tumor efficacy that may lead to decreased pain due to DT but has associated adverse reactions that require medical management. Patients in the nirogacestat arm appeared to require more treatment for infections compared to patients in the placebo arm, but it is reassuring that the rate of serious infections requiring treatment was similar across both arms.

L846: FDA also analyzed the use of concomitant medications that have potential anti-DT effects (e.g., based on literature), or whose mechanism of action affects the reproductive hormone levels (see table below). Patients in the nirogacestat arm were less likely to receive medications with potential (reported) anti-DT efficacy, so it is unlikely that use of these concomitant medications detracts from the anti-DT efficacy demonstrated by nirogacestat in Study NIR-DT-301. The concomitant medications whose mechanism of action affects the reproductive hormones were estrogen- and progesterone-analogues, and clomiphene, none of which are known to have anti-DT efficacy. See Section 8.1.2 for the efficacy subgroup analysis for patients who received these concomitant medications.

L852: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that patients in the nirogacestat arm underwent more concomitant procedures than the patients in the placebo arm. However, similar numbers of patients underwent DT-specific concomitant procedures across both arms, which were excision, nerve block or neurolysis, and radiotherapy (see Table below). The most common DT-specific procedure was excision of a mass, and all excisions were considered therapeutic surgeries by the Applicant. The other two procedures (nerve block or neurolysis, and radiotherapy) were not considered therapeutic procedures by the Applicant. Tumor pain was the most common reason for a DT-specific procedure (n=1 in nirogacestat, and n=2 in the
L853: placebo arm), and the patients were treated with nerve block or neurolysis (n=2, one patient in each arm) or tumor excision (n=1 in placebo arm) to treat the pain.
L854: The use of DT-specific concomitant procedures in Study NIR-DT-301 is unlikely to affect the outcomes of this trial since the number of procedures that were considered therapeutic, i.e., excisions, were balanced across the arms. The ADPR database (database for procedures) did not specify if the procedures were performed on target lesions or non-target lesions, but only 1 patient among the patients who underwent DT-specific excision or DT-specific radiotherapy had a response: patient ID (b) (6) in the nirogacestat arm had a partial response, and this patient underwent an excision of an osteoma of the jaw, which is not a DT. FDA also conducted a sensitivity analysis for PFS that excluded the patients who underwent the excisions for DT (n=5) and radiotherapy (n=1) from the PP population (i.e., revised PP population), and concluded that the revised PP population had a similar PFS to the ITT population (see “Protocol Violations/Deviations” in Section 8.1.2). In addition, the number of patients who underwent excisions or radiotherapy was balanced across the two arms.

L859: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L860: ## Data:
L861: A summary of PFS from randomization is presented in Table 35. A Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS from randomization is presented in Figure 2.
L862: The number of PFS events were lower in nirogacestat compared to placebo, leading to significantly higher PFS in the nirogacestat arm (12 participants [17%] with an event) versus the placebo arm (37 participants [51%] with an event), demonstrating a 71% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death (HR = 0.29 [95% CI: 0.15, 0.55]; p &lt; 0.001).
L863: Assessment of qualified clinical progression events were included in the analysis of PFS. Radiographic progression and qualified clinical progression each occurred at a lower rate in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm (16% vs 42% and 1% vs 8%, respectively). For both treatment arms, the majority of participants with a progression event had radiographic progression (11 of 12 participants in the nirogacestat arm; 30 of 36 participants in the placebo arm). A sensitivity analysis excluding events of qualified clinical progression found results consistent with the primary analysis and supported the inclusion of these events in the analysis of PFS (HR = 0.31 [95% CI: 0.15, 0.62]; p &lt; 0.001).

L878: ## The Applicant's Position:
L879: A statistically and clinically significant improvement in the primary endpoint of PFS was observed for nirogacestat over placebo, with a 71% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death (HR = 0.29 [95% CI: 0.15, 0.55]; p &lt; 0.001). The probability of being event free was higher in the nirogacestat arm compared to the placebo arm at Months 6 (91.8% vs 69.8%), 12 (84.7% vs 52.8%), and 24 (76.4% vs 44.0%), supporting a sustained clinical benefit over time.
L880: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L881: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that nirogacestat led to a statistically and clinically significant improvement in PFS in patients with DT compared to placebo, where PFS included both clinical and radiographic progression as assessed by blinded independent review. The median PFS was non-evaluable (NE, 95% CI: NE, NE) for patients on nirogacestat, and 15.1 months (95% CI: 8.4, NE) for patients on placebo, with a hazard ratio of 0.29 (95% CI: 0.15, 0.55; p-value &lt;0.001). Due to the discrepancy identified by the OSI (see Section 4.1), an updated figure of Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS is presented below. Only a censored date for PFS in 1 patient was affected. Therefore, the PFS HR and medians in arms remained the same, and the Kaplan-Meier plot of PFS is generally consistent with the original plot reported by the Applicant.
L882: **Figure 3:** Corrected Kaplan Meier Plot of PFS from Randomization – Double Blind Phase (ITT Population)

L884: Treatment benefit in PFS for nirogacestat was generally consistent across all subgroups analyzed (Figure 4).
L885: **Figure 4: Subgroup Analysis of PFS**
L886: Source: ADPR (SDN 39; May 5, 2023)
L887: Investigators determined that 9 patients had clinical progression, among whom the independent blinded central clinical review committee determined that 7 patients qualified for inclusion in the primary analysis for PFS (n=1 nirogacestat, n=6 placebo), and 2 did not qualify (n=1 for each arm). FDA's summaries of the narratives for clinical progression (qualified and unqualified) are presented in Table 36. Although the final assessments of the independent blinded central clinical review committee were reasonable for these patients, it is difficult to assess for bias (in the determination of clinical progression) because the committee was not necessarily reviewing clinical data for all patients in the trial in a real-time, continuous basis.
L888: Since clinical progression is a more subjective endpoint than radiographic progression, it was reassuring that nirogacestat also demonstrated improved PFS based on radiographic assessment alone compared to placebo, with a median PFS of NE (95% CI: NE, NE), and NE (95% CI: 11.0, NE), with a hazard ratio of 0.31 (95% CI: 0.16, 0.62). This sensitivity analysis in combination with ORR results and PRO analyses (see section below for Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints and section for Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA [PRO] endpoints) provided confidence that nirogacestat provides benefit to patients, overcoming the weakness of the primary endpoint in Study NIR-DT-301. Note that although the determinations of clinical progression below appeared reasonable, information was not provided (particularly for patients in the experimental arm) regarding whether there was bias in delaying a determination for a clinical progression event.

(b)(6): nirogacestat – 51 yo M with previously treated UF DT/AF located in the mesentery and pelvis, who enrolled in NIR-DT-301 14.11 years from diagnosis. Previous treatment was surgery. The Investigator determined that the patient had clinical progression on Trial Day 43, based on hematuria (onset Day 43), abdominal pain, postrenal kidney failure (onset Day 45), hemorrhagic anemia, and left-sided hydronephrosis (onset Day 45). Nirogacestat was discontinued due to “deterioration of health status,” and the last dose of nirogacestat was on Day 43. The patient subsequently developed intestinal obstruction (Trial Day not provided in narrative), and the Investigator noted progression of the pelvis tumor, and vital structure involvement, including bladder, kidney, and intestinal tract. The local RECIST v1.1 read and central imaging review both reported stable disease while the patient was on nirogacestat. The patient started an alternative therapy on the same day as the last dose of nirogacestat.

L903: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L904: ## Data:
L905: All participant data relating to the study was entered into the electronic CRFs unless transmitted to the sponsor or designee electronically (e.g., laboratory data).
L906: The investigator was responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct by electronically signing the CRF. The investigator maintained accurate documentation (source data) that supported the information entered in the CRF. The investigator permitted study-related monitoring, audits, Institutional Review Board (IRB) / Independent Ethics Committee (IEC) review, and regulatory agency inspections and provided direct access to source data documents.
L907: Monitoring details describing strategy methods, responsibilities, and requirements, including handling of noncompliance issues and monitoring techniques (on-site, remote, or central monitoring) were indicated in the monitoring plan to ensure the protocol and GCP was followed.
L908: The sponsor or designee was responsible for the data management of this study including quality checking of the data. The sponsor assumed accountability for actions delegated to other individuals (e.g., Contract Research Organizations).
L909: Study monitors performed ongoing source data verification to confirm that data entered into the eCRF by authorized site personnel were accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents; that the safety and rights of participants were being protected; and that the study was being conducted in accordance with the currently approved protocol and any other study agreements, ICH GCP, and all applicable regulatory requirements.
L910: Records and documents, including signed ICFs, pertaining to the conduct of this study were retained by the investigator according to specifications in the ICH guidelines, local regulations, or as specified in the clinical trial agreement, whichever was longer. No records were to be destroyed during the retention period without the written approval of the sponsor. No records were to be transferred to another location or party without written notification to the sponsor.
L911: SpringWorks conducts clinical studies according to procedures that incorporate the ethical principles of GCP. To ensure compliance with these procedures and to assess the adequacy of quality control procedures, SpringWorks undertakes a GCP audit program.
L912: Audits were performed by SpringWorks Quality Assurance (or designees) operating independently of the study monitors. The audits within the clinical program were aimed at study documentation, investigator sites, and CSRs.
L913: The audit program, together with SpringWorks' internal quality control procedures, provide reassurance that study conclusions were based on valid procedures for data management and analysis, and that the clinical study program was carried out in accordance with GCP guidelines.
L914: ## The Applicant's Position:
L915: No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent an adequate assessment of the data, or change the conclusions drawn.
L916: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L917: FDA agrees that Applicant has provided all the datasets and supporting metadata documentation required to validate the results. In addition, FDA conducted a random audit of 10% of the CRFs for Study NIR-DT-301 and verified that the information provided in the CRFs matched with the data presented in the datasets during this audit. See Section 434.1 (Office of Scientific Investigations) for details regarding the results of the FDA inspections.
L918: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L919: ## Data
L920: A summary of ORR is presented in Table 37. ORR was calculated based on the confirmed best overall response per central review. ORR was significantly higher (p &lt; 0.001) in the nirogacestat arm (29 participants [41%]) versus the placebo arm (6 participants [8%]). The ORR in the placebo arm (8%) was lower than the previously reported (20%) in another Phase 3 study of sorafenib in participants with DT (Gounder et al. 2018), which likely reflected the eligibility requirement for RECIST v1.1 progression in the participants in this study.
L921: The complete response (CR) rate was higher in the nirogacestat arm (5 participants [7%]) versus the placebo arm (no participants) (Table 37). The disease control rate (DCR) was similar between the treatment arms (91% vs 85%), which was due to the higher rate of stable disease in the placebo arm.

L930: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L931: The ORR was significantly higher (p &lt; 0.001) in the nirogacestat arm (29 participants [41%]) versus the placebo arm (6 participants [8%]), including 5 (7%) participants with a CR in the nirogacestat arm versus no participants with a CR in the placebo arm. The DCR was similar between the treatment arms (91% vs 85%), which may have been due to the higher rate of stable disease in the placebo arm. Median time to objective response was shorter for participants receiving nirogacestat as compared to placebo at 5.55 and 11.14 months, respectively.
L932: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L933: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Nirogacestat has direct anti-DT effect based on the difference in ORR between the two treatment arms. FDA also agrees that the difference in ORR between in the placebo arms of Study NIR-DT-301 and the trial conducted by *Gounder et al.* is likely due to the latter trial having a less restrictive criteria for radiographic progression for enrollment (i.e., \ge 10\% progression), and permitted enrollment of patients who did not have radiographic progression but had DT that was deemed inoperable or would require extensive surgery, or had symptomatic disease.
L934: FDA also notes that the 95% CI for ORR and DCR in nirogacestat arm are different from FDA's analyses. FDA used Clopper-Pearson Method, an exact method, to calculate the 95% CI for response rate. The 95% CI of ORR in nirogacestat is (29.8, 53.8), and the 95% CI of DCR in nirogacestat is (82.3, 96.8) from FDA's analyses. FDA's calculation of 95% CI for both ORR and DCR in the placebo arm are the same as the numbers reported by the Applicant. Since the DCR was not pre-specified in the hierarchical testing plan, FDA considers the reported p-value for DCR was nominal.
L935: ## **Dose/Dose Response**
L936: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L937: All participants were dosed at 150 mg BID; therefore, this is not applicable for this submission.
L938: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L939: See Section 6 of this review.
L940: ## Durability of Response
L941: ## Data:
L942: Median duration of response (DOR) for participants with CR or partial response (PR) was not reached. Similarly, median duration of stable disease (DoSD) for participants with CR, PR, or SD was not reached.
L943: ## The Applicant's Position:
L944: The Kaplan-Meier median DOR and median DoSD could not be estimated for either treatment arm due to the low number of responses with subsequent progressions or deaths on either arm.
L945: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L946: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The median DOR was not reached for both arms, while the range of the DOR is (2.8, 27.7+) months in nirogacestat and (2.8, 16.4+) months in the placebo.
L947: ## Persistence of Effect
L948: ## Data:
L949: Refer to Table 35 and Figure 2.
L950: ## The Applicant's Position:
L951: Refer to the primary endpoint position.
L952: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L953: The anti-DT effect of nirogacestat appears durable based on the probability of patients not having a progression event at 2 years from initiation of nirogacestat (76% [95% CI: 61, 87]) compared to placebo (44% [95% CI: 32, 56]) and the difference in median PFS between nirogacestat (15 months [95% CI: 8.4, not evaluable]) and placebo (not evaluable) (Table 35 and Figure 4).
L954: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L955: ## Data:
L956: As per the testing hierarchy for PRO assessments, Cycle 10 results are the secondary endpoints while overall results are supportive information (Figure 5).
L957: - For the BPI average pain intensity endpoint, least squares (LS) mean scores were significantly and clinically improved from Baseline in the nirogacestat arm versus the
L958: placebo arm at Cycle 10 (-1.547 vs -0.044; LS mean difference = -1.503; p &lt; 0.001) and overall (-1.566 vs -0.253; LS mean difference = -1.314; p &lt; 0.001).

- For the Gounder/DTRF Desmoid Symptom Scale GODDESS Desmoid Tumor Symptom Scale (DTSS) total symptom score (TSS) endpoint, the LS mean change from Baseline scores were significantly and clinically improved in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm at Cycle 10 (-1.200 vs 0.394; LS mean difference = -1.594; p &lt; 0.001) and overall (-1.237 vs 0.185; LS mean difference = -1.422; p &lt; 0.001).
- For the PRO GODDESS assessment of GODDESS Desmoid Tumor Impact Scale (DTIS) Physical Functioning domain endpoint, the LS mean change from Baseline scores were significantly and clinically improved in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm at Cycle 10 (-0.667 vs 0.161; LS mean difference = -0.829; p &lt; 0.001) and overall (-0.635 vs 0.005; LS mean difference = -0.640; p &lt; 0.001).
- For the EORTC QLQ-C30 Global Health Status/Quality of Life (GHS/QoL) endpoint, the LS mean change from Baseline scores were significantly and clinically improved at Cycle 10 in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm at Cycle 10 (4.473 vs -5.882; LS mean difference = 10.355; p = 0.007) and overall (2.023 vs -2.150; LS mean difference = 4.172; p = 0.035).
- LS mean change from Baseline EORTC QLQ-C30 Physical Functioning domain scores were significantly and clinically improved in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm at Cycle 10 (8.894 vs -4.558; LS mean difference = 13.452; p &lt; 0.001) and overall (6.836 vs -1.158; LS mean difference = 7.994; p &lt; 0.001).
- LS mean change from Baseline EORTC QLQ-C30 Role Functioning domain scores were significantly and clinically improved in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm at Cycle 10 (11.348 vs -5.445; LS mean difference = 16.793; p &lt; 0.001) and overall (10.008 vs -1.812; LS mean difference = 11.820; p &lt; 0.001).
L959: ## NDA/IND Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 217677
## Nirogacestat (PF-03084014)
L960: Figure 5: Mixed Model Repeat Measures: Change from Baseline in PRO Assessments (ITT Population)
L961: ## The Applicant's Position:
L962: Clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvements were observed with nirogacestat treatment for all PRO assessments evaluated as part of the secondary endpoints at the prespecified timepoint of Cycle 10. Statistically and clinically significant improvements occurred as early as Cycle 2 and were generally sustained throughout the DB phase (Osoba et al. 1998; Mease et al. 2011). Overall, results from the PRO analyses demonstrated improved health-related quality of life (HRQoL) with nirogacestat as compared to placebo. These findings are notable in DT which has been associated with substantial morbidity including pain and impaired physical functioning (Constantinidou et al. 2012; Quintini et al. 2012; Penel et al. 2017; Skubitz 2017; Gounder et al. 2020; Schut et al. 2022). Improvements in patient-reported symptoms and impacts have been specifically cited by the Desmoid Tumor Working Group (DTWG) as a treatment goal in their consensus-based guidelines on the management of DT (DTWG 2020) and highlight the benefit of nirogacestat for patients. The early and consistent benefits observed across patient-reported symptoms and impacts contribute to the robust radiographic findings and demonstrate a clear benefit for patients.
L963: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L964: The GODDESS (DTSS and DTIS), BPI-SF and EORTC QLQ-C30 instruments were used to collect PROs as secondary endpoints. The average pain intensity (API) score was derived using the average of the daily BPI-SF Item 3 score ("Worst Pain") over the 7-day period prior to each visit with range from 0 to 10, where higher scores indicate greater pain intensity. The DTSS TSS was calculated as the mean of the daily average TSS over a 7-day period if at least 4 out of 7 days were non-missing. The daily average TSS was calculated by averaging the three pain items (items 1-3) first and then averaging with items 4-7. Higher scores indicate greater symptom severity. DTIS Physical Functioning domain was calculated by averaging items 1, 2, 6, 7, and 8 with a 7-day recall period. Higher scores indicate worse physical functioning. The EORTC QLQ-C30 Physical Functioning, Role Functioning, and GHS/QoL domain scores were scored according to the EORTC scoring instructions (i.e., the average of the items that contribute to each scale taken as the raw score). Raw scores are linearly transformed to a 0-100 scale, where higher scores indicate a higher ("better") level of functioning or quality of life. PRO endpoints (mean change from baseline at cycle 10) were formally tested in the following order: BPI-SF API, DTSS, DTIS, EORTC QLQ-C30 GHS/QoL domain, EORTC QLQ-C30 Physical Functioning domain, and EORTC QLQ-C30 Role Functioning domain.
L965: The PRO results reported by the Applicant in the CSR and in the section above were calculated using all data from baseline to cycle 25, which was different from the pre-specified analysis method as described in the SAP. In response to an FDA information request, the Applicant provided corrected analyses on April 21, 2023, redoing the PRO analyses based on data from
L966: baseline to Cycle 10, as pre-specified in the SAP. See Table 38 below for the Applicant's corrected PRO endpoint results. The corrected PRO results were generally consistent with the PRO results reported in the CSR.

L970: FDA considers the EORTC QLQ-C30 GHS/QoL scale (items 29 and 30) as a broad measure that can be impacted by nontreatment/non-disease factors and therefore difficult to interpret. Therefore, FDA considered GHS/QoL to be exploratory and focused its review on the other secondary PRO endpoint results.
L971: In general, FDA concluded that the results from the pre-specified PRO endpoints were challenging to interpret for the following reasons:
L972: ## 1. There were issues with comprehensibility of the DTSS and DTIS:
L973: A definitive conclusion could not be made whether the DTSS and DTIS measures were well-understood and interpreted appropriately due to the limited approach used by the Applicant to evaluate participant comprehension. Specifically, the Applicant did not:
L974: - query participants about understanding and appropriateness of response options for all DTSS and DTIS items, but rather at time of first appearance of the response option type (i.e., queried participants only on DTSS Item 1 and DTIS item 1)
- consistently query participants about recall period across instructions and items.
2. There was substantial amount of missing data and differential missing data between treatment arms which makes it difficult to reliably interpret the PRO results in terms of efficacy:
L975: The available data rates (number of PRO completed/ITT population) at baseline and cycle 10 among PRO endpoints are summarized in Table 39 below. Figure 6 also shows the available data rate and reasons for data missingness over time using EORTC QLQ-C30 as an example.
