L979: **Figure 6:** Available Data Rate and Missing Reasons for EORTC QLQ-C30
L980: Source: IR response provided on 04/21/2023
L981: Using EORTC QLQ-C30 as an example: Available data rates were high at baseline for both nirogacestat and placebo arms; however, less than half of randomized patients were included in the analysis at cycle 10 (Table 39). The available data rate remained above 70% at Cycle 2 and Cycle 3, dropped to 50% from cycle 4 to cycle 7, and decreased to 40% at cycle 8-10 (Figure 6). In addition, the available data rates were differential at cycle 10. The high missing data and differential missing rate may not allow for reliable estimation of clinical benefit from nirogacestat when evaluating PROs using mean change from baseline at cycle 10.
L982: 3. There was an insufficient level of symptom and physical function impairment at baseline:
L983: Based on item-level distributional data from Study NIR-DT-301,
L984: - &gt;20% of participants endorsed the least severe response category (i.e., participants reported “None” as it related to their symptom experience) for the majority of the items in the DTSS TSS (n= 7/11, 63.6%).
- &gt;20% of participants endorsed the least severe response category at baseline (i.e., participants reported “None” as it related to their physical function experience) for the majority of the items in the DTIS Physical Functioning domain (n= 7/9, 77.8%).
L985: Because of limited impairment, there was no to very little room for improvement to occur in the trial for the DTSS and DTIS scores.
L986: ## Evaluation of BPI-SF API
L987: Although available data rates for BPI-SF at cycle 10 were low, FDA noted higher available data rates for BPI-SF at cycle 2 and cycle 3 (&gt; 80%). Also, because of the issues outlined above with the DTSS and DTIS, FDA performed additional descriptive analyses on the BPI results. FDA performed additional analyses on change from baseline at cycle 2 and change from baseline at cycle 3 on BPI-SF API to further evaluate the pain improvement for patients in the study. For patients who reported pain at baseline with API \ge2 score, the pain improvement at cycle 2 was 2.7-points and 1-point in the nirogacestat and placebo arms, respectively. Similarly, for the same group of patients, pain improved by 3.5-points in the nirogacestat arm and 1.2-points in the placebo arm at cycle 3.
L988: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L989: ## Data:
L990: Analysis of exploratory efficacy endpoints showed the following:
L991: - Mean tumor volumes were larger in the nirogacestat arm (421.166 mL vs 371.999 mL) at Baseline as compared to the placebo arm. There were significantly greater decreases in tumor volume in the nirogacestat arm than in the placebo arm at Cycles 7, 13, 19, 25, and 31 (p &lt; 0.003), and numerically smaller tumor volume at EOT (p = 0.065).
- Mean MRI T2 hyperintensity was numerically more reduced from Baseline in the nirogacestat arm than the placebo arm at Cycles 7, 13, 25, and 31, and at EOT. A total of 23 participants receiving nirogacestat reported a change in T2 hyperintensity from &gt; 90% to &lt; 90%, which has been shown to be prognostic in participants with DT (Cassidy et al. 2020).
- Efficacy analyses were performed within the nirogacestat arm for participants who had a dose reduction to 100 mg BID versus those who did not have a dose reduction. There was no notable difference in PFS and ORR between participants who dose reduced versus those who did not. PRO analyses of BPI, DTSS, DTIS, and EORTC showed no apparent loss of efficacy with a dose reduction to 100 mg BID.The genetics exploratory endpoint of frequency and distribution of each mutation (*APC* and *CTNNB1* genes) showed that among the 105 participants with evaluable tumor samples, 85 participants (81%) had a *CTNNB1* mutation and 22 participants (21%) had an *APC* mutation. Mutation frequencies were similar between treatment arms and generally consistent with the expected disease etiology although a slightly higher percentage of participants had *APC* mutations
L992: than previously reported in the literature (Kasper et al. 2011; Riedel &amp; Agulnik 2022). There were no apparent correlations between identified genetic mutations and PFS, suggesting that nirogacestat is an effective treatment for DT regardless of genetic mutation.
L993: - Exploratory analyses of PRO endpoints supported the clinically meaningful benefit of nirogacestat.
L994: ## The Applicant's Position:
L995: Given the limitations of RECIST in DT, other imaging modalities were evaluated as exploratory endpoints including volumetric MRI and T2 hyperintensity. These supportive analyses demonstrated a greater reduction in tumor volume and T2 hyperintensity among participants receiving nirogacestat as compared to placebo and may represent a more precise measure of tumor changes in DT as compared to RECIST v1.1 (Kummar et al. 2017). Volumetric MRI may also allow an earlier estimate of disease progression than RECIST v1.1 (Lefkowitz 2015; DTWG 2020; Subhawong et al. 2021). Data from the volumetric MRI and T2 hypersensitivity analyses provide additional radiographic evidence supporting the benefit of nirogacestat in the treatment of DT as compared to placebo.
L996: Exploratory analyses did not show differences in PFS, ORR, or PRO endpoints between participants who dose reduced to 100 mg BID versus those who did not have a dose reduction.
L997: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L998: No difference in efficacy was detected between the subgroup of patients who reduced the dose of nirogacestat to 100 mg BID and the subgroup who did not have a dose reduction; however, the analysis of PFS considering dose reduction should be interpreted with caution because there is not a comparable group in the control arm (and, more importantly, the analyses would have been a post randomization analysis). Note that the p-values reported for exploratory endpoints in this section were considered nominal.
L999: FDA interpretation of the statement regarding PRO analyses (in Data above) is that these analyses provided support for the primary assessment of PFS and ORR. The PRO analyses, however, have limitations as described in FDA's review above.
L1000: ## 8.1.4. Study A8641014
L1001: ## 8.1.5. Trial Design
L1002: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1003: Study A8641014 was an open-label, non-randomized, Phase 1 dose-finding study (Messersmith et al. 2015) in participants with advanced solid tumors conducted to determine the MTD and
L1004: recommended Phase 2 dose (RP2D) for the future clinical development of nirogacestat. In the dose-finding portion of the study, the MTD of nirogacestat administered BID continuously for 21 days was established at 220 mg BID in participants with advanced solid tumors. Additional participants were subsequently enrolled in the expansion cohort at 150 mg or 220 mg BID. The RP2D in participants with advanced solid tumors, including DT, was determined to be 150 mg BID by comparing the tolerability, PK, and PDx profile of nirogacestat at these 2 doses.
L1005: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1006: Study A8641014 was a first-in-patient, multicenter, open-label, non-randomized, dose-finding Phase 1a/1b trial that employed a 3+3 dose-escalation design. The primary objective in the original protocol was to determine the MTD of nirogacestat BID as a single agent or in combination with dexamethasone in patients with advanced solid tumors. In the Phase 1b portion of the trial, the primary objective was to determine the safety and tolerability of nirogacestat at the MTD in patients with advanced solid tumors and acute T-cell lymphoblastic leukemia/lymphoblastic lymphoma (T-ALL/LBL). Amendment 6 (dated October 26, 2011) of the protocol removed the plan to investigate nirogacestat with dexamethasone from both the dose-escalation and dose-expansion portions of the trial because after analysis of the data of nirogacestat as a single-agent, the Applicant decided that the addition dexamethasone was not necessary for patients with advanced solid tumors.
L1007: Eight patients with T-ALL/LBL and 64 patients with advanced solid tumors received nirogacestat on this trial. Based on the results of the Phase 1a portion of the trial, the Applicant determined that the MTD of nirogacestat was 220 mg BID for patients with advanced tumors and investigated nirogacestat 150 mg BID and 220 mg BID in the Phase 1b portion of the trial. The Applicant determined that there were more treatment-related Grade 3 TEAEs with the 220 mg BID dose compared to the 150 mg BID and chose nirogacestat 150 mg BID as the RP2D for patients with advanced solid tumors. The MTD was not determined for patients with T-ALL/LBL because dose escalation was terminated early due to low enrollment rate and early data suggesting that the proof-of-mechanism was unlikely to be achieved in T-ALL/LBL.
L1008: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1009: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L1010: Key inclusion criteria included:
L1011: - Men and women \ge 16 years old.
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status of:0 or 1 for participants with advanced solid tumor.
- 0, 1, or 2 for participants with refractory or relapsed T-ALL.
L1012: - Participants with advanced solid tumor malignancy (with measurable disease as per RECIST v1.0 criteria for participants enrolled in the expansion cohort) that was resistant to standard therapy or for which no standard therapy was available, or
- Participant was &gt; 3 weeks (advanced solid tumor malignancy) or &gt; 2 weeks (T-ALL/LBL) from last anticancer treatment (except hydroxyurea, and intrathecal prophylaxis in T-ALL population) at study entry and all acute adverse events (AEs) of any prior therapy had resolved or, if persisting, were not considered to be clinically important.
L1013: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1014: Patients with T-ALL/LBL must have had refractory disease, disease that was resistant to current treatment options, or had no standard therapy available. Patients who previously had their cancer treated with a gamma secretase inhibitor were not eligible. Patients taking tamoxifen were excluded. Patients with the following diseases were also excluded: clinical evidence of CNS disease, active hepatitis B infection, hepatitis C infection, or known HIV or acquired immunodeficiency syndrome (AIDS)-related illness.
L1015: ## **Study Efficacy Endpoints**
L1016: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1017: Primary:
L1018: No efficacy endpoint was defined as primary endpoint in this study.
L1019: Secondary:
L1020: - Objective response (OR) was defined as confirmed CR or confirmed PR according to RECIST (version 1.0).
- Time to tumor progression (TTP) was defined as the time from Cycle 1 Day 1 to date of first documentation of objective progression.
- DOR was only defined for participants with an OR. It was defined as the time from start date (date of first documentation of PR or CR) to date of first documentation of objective progression or death
- PFS was defined as the time from Cycle 1 Day 1 to date of first documentation of progression or death due to any cause.
- Time to response (TTR), defined as the time from first date of receiving study treatment until the first OR date for participants whose best overall response is a CR or PR.
L1021: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1022: FDA would like to clarify that evaluation of TTP, TTR, and PFS were included as a secondary endpoints in the protocol for Study A8641014. However, time-to-event endpoints are not
L1023: interpretable in nonrandomized trials, and the primary objective of this trial was to determine the MTD, not the efficacy of nirogacestat.
L1024: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L1025: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1026: The final statistical analysis plan (SAP), Amendment 3, was dated 26 March 2012.
L1027: The number of participants to be enrolled in the study depended on the observed safety profile, which was to determine the number of participants per dose level and the number of dosing cohorts in the study. Each dose level in the dose escalating components was to enroll at least 3, and up to 6 DLT eligible participants. Once the MTD for solid tumors had been determined, approximately 22 additional participants with advanced solid tumor, including participants with DT, soft tissue sarcoma, lung cancer, breast cancer and other solid tumors, were enrolled to help determine RP2D.
L1028: All enrolled participants who were eligible, received study treatment, and had baseline assessments and at least 1 on study tumor assessment prior to any new anticancer therapies were considered evaluable for response. Participants who were treated and discontinued from treatment prior to on study tumor assessment because of clinical disease progression were considered evaluable for efficacy and counted as failures.
L1029: Due to the exploratory nature of the study, no confirmatory inferential analyses were planned. Summary tables were produced by dose level. If there were sufficient numbers of participants accrued in certain malignancies, the efficacy endpoints were also to be tabulated for those malignancies separately. Descriptive statistics such as means, medians, standard deviations and ranges for continuous data and frequencies and percentages for categorical data were presented. Tumor response was tabulated and displayed graphically, where appropriate.
L1030: A point estimate of the ORR and a 2-sided 95% confidence interval (CI) were calculated using an exact method. Survival endpoints, i.e., TTP, TTR, duration of response, and PFS, were summarized using the Kaplan-Meier method, and the Brookmeyer Crowley method was used to calculate the 95% CI for the median.
L1031: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1032: FDA agrees that this study should be considered exploratory and supportive. The primary efficacy assessment supporting the approval of nirogacestat for patients with progressing DTs was based on Study NIR-DT-301.
L1033: ## Protocol Amendments
L1034: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1035: The original protocol dated 15 December 2008 was amended 8 times. None of the amendments impacted the integrity of quality of the study and/or its outcomes.
L1036: This study was originally designed to have 4 additional components: an expansion cohort in participants with T-ALL/LBL; an optional dose-finding component in participants with solid tumor for combinational treatment of nirogacestat plus dexamethasone; an optional dose-finding component in participants with T-ALL/LBL for combinational treatment of nirogacestat plus dexamethasone; and an optional expansion component in participants with advanced solid tumor for combinational treatment of nirogacestat plus dexamethasone. These components were later removed due to operational and scientific reasons. As agreed upon at the Pre-NDA meeting, participants with T-ALL/LBL were excluded from the integrated safety analyses as these hematologic malignancies were anticipated to be sufficiently different from DT to preclude any comparisons regarding safety data.
L1037: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1038: FDA outlines below the key protocol amendments that pertained to the advanced solid tumor cohorts. Thus, it excludes descriptions of Amendment 7 (dated February 2, 2012) and Amendment 8 (dated May 14, 2012) whose revisions pertained to the T-ALL/LBL cohorts, including halting of enrollment into the T-ALL/LBL cohorts in Amendment 8 due to low enrollment rate, and early data suggesting that proof-of-concept mechanism unlikely to be achieved for T-ALL/LBL. The following amendments are also not described below because the revisions were not substantive and did not lead to major changes to the trial conduct or trial objectives: Amendment 2 (dated March 11, 2010), Amendment 4 (dated August 27, 2010), and Amendment 9 (dated April 19, 2013). All the amendments were activated except for Amendment 3; Amendment 4 was activated, and it was similar to Amendment 3. The original protocol was dated December 15, 2008.
L1039: Amendment 1 (dated November 5, 2009)
L1040: - The Applicant changed the dosing and schedule of dexamethasone.
- The Applicant allowed enrollment of patients who were 16 years old (previously enrolled only patients who were older than 16 years old).
L1041: Amendment 3 (dated August 16, 2010)
L1042: - The Applicant amended the protocol to focus on characterizing the safety and tolerability of nirogacestat as a single-agent.
- The Applicant added a food effect analysis for patients with solid tumors treated at the MTD in the expansion cohort.
L1043: ## Amendment 5 (dated May 2, 2011)
L1044: - The Applicant increased the number of patients to be enrolled in the expansion cohort from 12 to approximately 22, and enrollment into this cohort was restricted to patients with measurable disease based on RECIST.
- The Applicant added collection of hair follicles and matched peripheral blood samples from patients with solid tumor for biomarker evaluation.
L1045: ## Amendment 6 (dated October 26, 2011)
L1046: - In the dose expansion cohort, the Applicant allowed enrollment of patients with solid tumors to dose levels lower than the MTD.
- The Applicant updated the list of tumors that may be enrolled into the dose expansion cohorts.
- The Applicant removed the plan to investigate nirogacestat with dexamethasone from both the dose-escalation and dose-expansion portions of the trial because after analysis of the data of nirogacestat as a single agent, the Applicant decided that the addition dexamethasone was not necessary for patients with advanced solid tumors.

In addition, the study will be conducted in accordance with the protocol, the International Conference on Harmonisation guideline on Good Clinical Practice, and applicable local regulatory requirements and laws."

L1073: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1074: FDA did not independently verify the results presented in this Assessment Aid by the Applicant for early phase trials, such as Study A8641014, because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat for the treatment of patients with progressing DTs is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301.
L1075: Based on the information presented by the Applicant on Table 71, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Most patients received the nirogacestat 150 mg BID dose (n=23), followed by 220 mg BID (n=16). The highest dose investigated was nirogacestat 330 mg BID (n=3). The most common reason for discontinuing nirogacestat at all dose levels was objective progression or relapse.
L1076: Among the 9 patients with DT, the reasons for discontinuation from treatment were withdrawal by patient (n=3), adverse event (n=2), unqualified clinical progression (n=1), and other (n=3).

L1082: Abbreviations: ICD: informed consent document.
L1083: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1084: A formal acknowledgement by the study team was made that deviations were reviewed and GCP compliance was maintained. None of the observed protocol deviations appeared to impact the conclusions of the CSR.
L1085: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1086: In addition to the protocol deviations the Applicant listed in Table 41, FDA considers the following protocol deviations important because an increased frequency of these deviations may impact the trial's ability to achieve its objective of determining the MTD, and the subsequent determination of the RP2D: patients were not adherent to documentation of nirogacestat administration (n=11/72, 15%), patients missed one or more doses of nirogacestat (n=7/72, 10%), tumor assessments were not conducted on one or more occasion (n=3/72, 4.2%), a trial clinical visit was not conducted (n=3/72, 4.2%), and end of treatment visit was not conducted (n=1/72, 1.4%).
L1087: Data from Study A8641014 were adequate for FDA to review the analysis that led to the Applicant's determination that the MTD for nirogacestat was 220 mg BID. FDA agrees that the MTD for nirogacestat appears to be 220 mg BID. Thus, the protocol deviations did not appear to impact the ability of Study A8641014 to achieve its objective.

L1094: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1095: The population in this study had a range of age, weight, and height that was appropriate for evaluation of nirogacestat in a Phase 1 dose-escalation study.
L1096: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1097: FDA did not independently verify the results presented in this Assessment Aid by the Applicant for early phase trials, such as Study A8641014, because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat for the treatment of patients with progressing DTs is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301.
L1098: Based on the results presented by the Applicant on Table 42, among the patients with solid tumors, the distribution of male and female patients was well-balanced, the mean weight reflected the U.S. population, and the spread of ages was appropriate for a first-in-patient trial. This trial, like Study NIR-DT-301, enrolled mostly White patients, and the proportion of non-White patients did not reflect the diversity of the U.S. population.
L1099: Among the 9 patients with DT, 5 patients were men, and 4 were women. The mean age was 32 years (standard deviation 6.9 years, range: 23-41 years). Eight patients were White, and 1 was Black. Eight patients were not Hispanic or Latino, and 1 patient was Hispanic or Latino. This was a small sample size, but the age distribution was reflective of the general population with DT. Due to the small sample size, the sex distribution was not reflective of the general population with DT. As stated above, the distribution of race and ethnicity in this trial was not reflective of the diversity of the U.S. population.

L1108: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1109: The study design included an expansion cohort that specified the inclusion of DT and soft tissue sarcomas, among other solid tumor types. The demographics reflect this study design feature.
L1110: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1111: The Applicant's Table 74 does not combine the diagnostic terms that may be a description of similar cancers. For example, in Study A8641014, 12 patients had a diagnosis of colorectal cancer (colon cancer [n=7], colorectal cancer [n=4], and rectal cancer [n=1]), 5 patients had a diagnosis of lung cancer (lung neoplasm malignant [n=3], non-small cell lung cancer [n=1], and small cell lung cancer [n=1]), 3 patients had liver cancer (hepatic cancer metastatic [n=1], and hepatic neoplasm malignant [n=2]), 3 patients had leiomyosarcoma (leiomyosarcoma at an unspecified site [n=2] or uterine leiomyosarcoma [n=1]), and 2 patients had kidney cancer (renal cancer [n=1] or renal cell carcinoma [n=1]). One patient each had a diagnosis of the remaining unlisted cancers.
L1112: Among the patients with DT, 1 patient had a diagnosis of FAP. The APC and CTNNB1 status of the tumors were unknown for all the patients. All 9 patients with DT had previous therapy: radiotherapy (n=9), systemic therapy (n=7), and surgery (n=6). None of the patients had previous treatment with a tyrosine kinase inhibitor, and 3 patients had prior treatment with chemotherapy.
L1113: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L1114: ## Data:
L1115: All participants received concomitant drug treatments during the study. The most commonly reported concomitant drug treatments included antiemetics and antinauseants (for example, prochlorperazine and ondansetron), anti-diarrheal (for example, diphenoxylate hydrochloride and atropine [Lomotil]) and analgesics (for example, oxycodone and ibuprofen).
L1116: A total of 9 solid tumor participants (1, 4, 3, and 1 in the 130 mg, 150 mg, 220 mg and 330 mg BID cohorts, respectively) had planned dose reductions and 3 of them had reductions in the first cycle. All of these dose reductions were caused by treatment-related AEs, which were Grades 1 to 3, not reported as SAEs, and resolved following temporary discontinuations or dose reductions.
L1117: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1118: Commonly reported concomitant drug treatments were appropriate supportive care for known adverse reactions to nirogacestat. Treatment compliance was high, with dose reductions caused by treatment-related AEs as mandated by the protocol.
L1119: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1120: Patients with DT were treated in the following dose levels of nirogacestat (all BID dosing): 80 mg (n=2), 150 mg (n=2), 220 mg (n=3), and 330 mg (n=2). One patient each had a dose reduction at the higher dose levels: 150 mg, 220 mg, and 330 mg. Patients with DT had a high relative dose intensities and long treatment exposures with nirogacestat (Table 44).

L1125: Among the 64 patients with solid tumors, the most common indications for concomitant drug treatments were pain (n=45, 70%), nausea or nausea prophylaxis (n=36, 56%), diarrhea or diarrhea prophylaxis (n=34, 53%), hypertension or hypertension prophylaxis (n=27, 42%), nutritional supplementation (n=27, 42%), constipation or constipation prophylaxis (n=25, 39%), acid reflux (n=23, 36%), hypophosphatemia or hypophosphatemia prophylaxis (n=19, 30%), insomnia (n=17, 27%), hypothyroidism (n=16, 25%), depression (n=16, 25%), and hypokalemia (n=14, 22%).
L1126: The most common indications for the use of concomitant medications in Study A8641014 were similar to those of Study NIR-DT-301, and consistent with the effects of the tumor (i.e., pain) or
L1127: with the adverse reactions associated with nirogacestat, such as nausea, diarrhea, and electrolyte loss. The remaining indications are for conditions that are common among the general population in the U.S., namely hypertension, constipation, acid reflux, insomnia, hypothyroidism, and depression. The Applicant did not submit the dataset for concomitant procedures for Study A8641014. However, because efficacy was not the primary objective of this trial, this dataset is not required for the review of the data from this trial.
L1128: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L1129: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1130: No efficacy endpoint was defined as primary endpoint in this study.
L1131: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1132: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1133: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L1134: ## Data:
L1135: No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent an adequate assessment of the data, and change the conclusions drawn.
L1136: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1137: All study sites were initiated during a sponsor investigator meeting or a site visit by the sponsor or designated representative. Each site was provided with an instructional manual to ensure the use of standard terminology and the collection of accurate, consistent, complete, and reliable data. Central laboratory services were used in this study for consistency.
L1138: The sponsor monitored the study through routine center visits. At these visits, study procedures were reviewed, CRF data compared to original clinical records, data queries resolved, and protocol deviations discussed with the investigator. Telephone and e-mail contact was maintained with the investigators between center visits. In addition, the overall study conduct was subject to internal quality review by the sponsor.
L1139: Quality assurance audits were performed at 2 sites by the sponsor's own independent quality assurance group under the group's discretion. These audits were conducted according to the sponsor's procedures and GCP guidelines.
L1140: The CSR was subject to quality control review in accordance with the sponsor's standard operating procedures (SOPs).
L1141: An independent third-party data monitoring committee was not established for this study. But an internal safety review team comprising physicians, safety specialists, and statisticians was set up to review individual and summary data collected in the safety and clinical databases periodically.
L1142: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1143: FDA agrees that Applicant has provided all the datasets and supporting metadata documentation required to validate the results.
L1144: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1145: ## Data:
L1146: There were 46 participants with solid tumors evaluable for response. Overall, the ORR (95% CI) was 13.0% (4.9%, 26.3%) for participants with solid tumors and 71.4% (95% CI [29.0%, 96.3%]) in participants with DT (Table 45). Six participants had an OR: 1 CR was in a participant with thyroid cancer and 5 out of 7 participants with DT had PRs. There were no other responders. As of the time of data cutoff, all 6 responders were censored in the calculation of DoR. All 5 DT responders had not progressed and were censored at the time of data cutoff, with 4 still on study and 1 discontinued due to non-compliance. The participant with thyroid cancer with a CR later had recurrence of disease but was censored at the last disease assessment of CR due to missed tumor assessments.
L1147: Median DoR for the 6 participants with ORs was not reached due to censoring, with a range of 1.7+ to 24.2+ months, at the time of data cutoff on 09 January 2013. Overall, the median (95% CI) of TTR for the 5 responders with DT was 8.5 (2.9, 30.4) months (Table 45).
L1148: For median (95% CI) of both TTP and PFS, the Kaplan-Meier estimates were 1.6 (1.4, 6.0) months for the 150 mg BID cohort and 1.5 (1.1, 4.2) months for the 220 mg BID cohort. The probability (95% CI) of TTP at 6 months and of PFS at 12 months for the 150 mg BID cohort was 33.6% (13.8%, 54.8%) and 26.8% (9.2%, 48.5%), respectively. The probability (95% CI) of TTP at 6 months for the 220 mg BID cohort was 20.0% (3.1%, 47.5%) but the probability of PFS at 12 months was not available for this dose level.

L1158: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1159: The clinical efficacy results showed encouraging activity of nirogacestat at the 150 mg and 220 mg dose level specifically in those participants with DT.
L1160: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1161: FDA did not independently verify the efficacy results because FDA considered Study NIR-DT-301 to be the primary basis for determining efficacy of nirogacestat. Responses were reported at all dose levels that were tested in patients with DT; therefore, FDA cannot determine based on this assessment in a limited number of patients which dose of nirogacestat is most promising for patients with DT. Refer to the clinical review of pharmacology for a more thorough discussion of the dose.
L1162: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1163: ## Data:
L1164: Table 45 shows the best overall response for participants with DT by dose group.
L1165: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1166: Responses in DT were seen across all dose levels spanning 80 mg BID to 330 mg BID, and therefore a conclusion on dose response cannot be drawn.
L1167: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1168: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that there were responses at dose levels spanning 80 mg BID to 330 mg BID. However, given the small number of patients per dose cohort in patients with DT, FDA cannot determine based on an assessment of this trial whether there is a dose-response relationship for patients with DT. Refer to clinical pharmacology review above for additional details regarding FDA assessment of the dosing regimen.
L1169: ## Durability of Response
L1170: ## Data:
L1171: Median DOR for participants with ORs was not reached.
L1172: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1173: Response was durable among participants with ORs.
L1174: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1175: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position, but this conclusion is limited by the small sample size of patients with DT in Study A8641014.
L1176: ## Persistence of Effect
L1177: ## Data:
L1178: The median (95% CI) PFS for participants with solid tumors was 1.6 (1.4, 4.2) months and the probability (95% CI) of PFS at 12 months was 29.8% (17.1%, 43.7%). The median (95% CI) of TTP for participants with solid tumors was 1.6 (1.4, 6.0) months for the 150 mg BID cohort and 1.5 (1.1, 4.2) months for the 220 mg BID cohort.
L1179: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1180: No obvious trend was observed in the analysis of TTP and PFS.
L1181: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1182: Time-to-event endpoints are not interpretable in nonrandomized trials. See FDA's Assessment regarding Durability of Response.

L1193: ## **8.1.7. Study 14-C-0007**
L1194: ## **8.1.8. Trial Design**
L1195: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1196: Study 14-C-0007 is an NCI-sponsored, open-label Phase 2 study conducted to determine the ORR (CR + PR) of nirogacestat in participants with DT; no randomization or blinding was performed. Nirogacestat was administered orally at a dose of 150 mg BID continuously in 21-day cycles. This is an ongoing study; results are based on a data cutoff of 04 November 2020.
L1197: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1198: FDA agrees with the Applicant's Description.
L1199: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1200: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1201: Key inclusion criteria included:
L1202: - Participants \ge 18 years of age with histologically confirmed DT confirmed by the Laboratory of Pathology, NCI, that progressed after receiving \ge 1 line of standard
L1203: treatment and that was not amenable to surgical resection or definitive radiation therapy.
L1204: - Had measurable disease by CT scan defined as those that could be accurately measured in at least one dimension (longest diameter to be recorded) as \ge 20 mm by chest x-ray, as \ge 10 mm with CT scan, or \ge 10 mm with calipers by clinical exam.
- Had ECOG performance status \le 2.
- Had a life expectancy &gt; 3 months with normal organ and marrow function.
L1205: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1206: FDA agrees with the Applicant's Description but clarifies that patients must not have been amenable to *curative* surgical resection to be eligible to enroll and must have had *adequate* organ and marrow function (e.g., patients with anemia or mild renal dysfunction were eligible to enroll if hemoglobin \ge 9 g/dL, or creatinine clearance \ge 60 mL/min/1.73 m^2 if creatinine levels &gt; 1.5 mg/dL, respectively). Patients who were previously treated with gamma secretase inhibitors or anti-Notch antibodies were not eligible to enroll. Patients with HIV who were being treated with combination antiretroviral therapy were ineligible.
L1207: ## **Study Efficacy Endpoints**
L1208: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1209: Primary:
L1210: - The primary endpoint was the ORR, defined as the proportion of participants who have objective response as CR or PR assessed by RECIST v1.1 using CT scans or MRI scans.
L1211: Secondary:
L1212: - ORR and DCR by mutation type of *APC* and *CTNNB1* genes.
L1213: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1214: FDA clarifies that Study 14-C-0007 did not have secondary endpoints, but did have exploratory objectives, which included correlation of response to nirogacestat with genotype.
L1215: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L1216: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1217: The original SAP, dated 08 December 2021, was amended once; the final version is dated 01 April 2022. As of the data cut, there were no changes between the protocol-defined statistical analyses and those presented in the SAP.
L1218: This study was conducted as a single stage Phase 2 study to rule out an unacceptably low 10% clinical response rate (PR + CR; p_0 = 0.10) in favor of a modestly high response rate of 35% (p_1 = 0.35). A type I error rate (\alpha, probability of accepting a poor treatment) of 0.10 with a response rate of 0.10 and a statistical power of 0.90 (\beta = 0.10, probability of rejecting a good treatment) when the true response rate was 0.35 was assumed, which justified a sample size of 17 participants. With 16 evaluable participants and all other assumptions the same, the statistical power is still above 0.90.
L1219: The analysis of disease response was derived based on actual tumor measurements reported in the eCRF. This analysis may have differed from the clinical review of RECIST, which was performed by the NCI during the conduct of the study and used to make treatment decisions on-study.
L1220: ORR was presented along with frequency for best overall response (BOR) and for confirmed BOR. The BOR was derived using tumor measurements per RECIST v1.1. BOR is defined as the best response recorded from the start of the treatment until disease progression or recurrence, in the order of CR, PR, stable disease (SD), not evaluable, and PD. The BOR was considered confirmed if both the measurement and confirmation criteria were achieved. The overall ORR was calculated using the confirmed BOR, which was based on the derived analysis of tumor response.
L1221: ORR for mutation type *APC* and *CTNNB1* genes were determined in the same manner as the primary analysis.
L1222: Important analyses performed by Kummar et al (2017) such as change in target tumor size, DOR, and DoSD were conducted for the data cut dates used in this interim CSR, for the purpose of updating and confirming the results. At the time of the interim CSR data cut, tumor volume data were not centrally databased through NCI, so this analysis was not performed for this interim report.
L1223: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1224: FDA did not independently verify the results presented in this Assessment Aid by the Applicant for early phase trials, such as Study 14-C-0007, because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat for the treatment of patients with progressing DTs is based on the pivotal trial NLR-DT-301.
L1225: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L1226: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1227: The study protocol was amended 12 times; the final protocol (Protocol Amendment 12) was dated 10 April 2020. Consistent with NCI standard practice, the protocol was amended
L1228: approximately annually. None of the amendments impacted the integrity of quality of the study and/or its outcomes.
L1229: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1230: There are 15 versions of Study 14-C-0007, Amendments A through O. The original protocol was dated October 15, 2013. Most of the amendments were limited to administrative changes to the protocol. The following amendments were noteworthy:
L1231: - Amendment H (dated April 27, 2018) – NCI clarified that a treatment cycle is considered complete if a patient received \ge 90\% of the planned dose of nirogacestat because “missing a small percentage of doses is unlikely to have a large effect on patients.”
- Amendment I (dated September 4, 2018) – NCI clarified that the correlative objectives for this trial are exploratory and changed them from secondary objectives to exploratory objectives.
- Amendment L (dated April 10, 2020) – added information regarding the risk of “primary ovarian insufficiency in women of childbearing potential based on an IND/CIOMS safety report from the randomized phase 3 trial of Nirogacestat”
L1232: ## 8.1.9. Study Results
L1233: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1234: ## Data
L1235: This study was conducted in accordance with the ethical principles of GCP and according to the ICH Harmonized Tripartite Guideline.
L1236: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1237: Study 14-C-0007 was GCP compliant.
L1238: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1239: The CSR for Study 14-C-0007 stated that the clinical trial was “designed in accordance with Good Clinical Practice as required by the major regulatory authorities and in accordance with the ethical principles of the Declaration of Helsinki.”
L1240: ## Financial Disclosure
L1241: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1242: The 14-C-0007 study was a single center study sponsored by the NCI. Consistent with NCI requirements, no investigators were identified as having financial interests/arrangements with the sponsor.
L1243: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1244: The data from Study 14-C-0007 is supportive of the pivotal data that supports this NDA, i.e., Study NIR-DT-301. As such, FDA did not require formal submission of the financial disclosure form for this supportive clinical trial.

L1260: Median (interquartile range), years	Mean (standard deviation), years
Patients who refused further treatment with nirogacestat, n=10	1.3 (0.7, 4.4)	2.4 (2.1)
Patients who had ongoing treatment with nirogacestat, n=5	6.6 (6.6, 6.8)	6.7 (0.1)
L1261: Source: ADSL (SDN 4; November 10, 2022)
L1262: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L1263: ## Data
L1264: Per the protocol, a deviation was defined as any change, divergence, or departure from the IRB approved study procedures in a research protocol.
L1265: There were two cases of incorrect dosing of nirogacestat by the participants. One participant was approved for a 1-week delay to the start of cycle due to the participant's work schedule. The participant reported using "leftover pills" to dose with nirogacestat on three days during the scheduled delay period. Another participant notified the research nurse that for the last 4 days of Cycle 5, the participant was out of town and did not take enough nirogacestat for the
L1266: duration of the trip. The participant took 100 mg nirogacestat BID instead of the prescribed 150 mg BID.
L1267: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1268: The deviations were reviewed and GCP compliance was maintained. None of the observed protocol deviations impacted the primary objective of the study and did not affect the overall safety and efficacy outcomes of the study.
L1269: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1270: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the protocol deviations do not affect the trial's ability to achieve its objective of determining the ORR in patients with DT. Based on the list of protocol deviations provided by the Applicant for Study 14-C-0007, only one patient had a delay in tumor imaging (one month delay not due to adverse events).
L1271: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the two dosing errors. In addition, there were 6 dosing delays, where 4 delays were due to adverse events, and 2 delays were not due to adverse events. The 4 adverse events that led to dose delays were motor vehicle accident, squamous cell carcinoma, swelling of clitoris, and bronchitis (n=1 per event). For the 2 dosing delays not related to adverse events, one patient did not receive nirogacestat as planned, and the other patient wanted to discuss with the Principal Investigator whether to continue treatment with nirogacestat. One patient was not adherent or inconsistent self-reporting of nirogacestat administration and returning leftover nirogacestat supply. These are minor protocol deviations and are unlikely to affect the trial's ability to determine the efficacy, safety, and tolerability of nirogacestat in patients with DT.
L1272: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L1273: ## Data:
L1274: Of the 17 participants, 3 (18%) were male and 14 (82%) were female. The median age at entry was 34 years (range: 19 to 69 years). The majority (15 [88%]) of participants reported race as white. A total of 9 participants [53%] had a CTNNB1 T41A tumor mutation, 3 participants (18%) had a CTNNB1 S45F tumor mutation, 2 participants (12%) had an APC germline tumor mutation, and 1 participant had an APC somatic tumor mutation. There were no cases of more than one mutation type reported per participant. Two participants (12%) reported no mutation types. Twelve (71%) participants had a single tumor, and 5 participants (29%) had multi-focal disease. Focality was determined programmatically based on the target tumor and imaging modality used for the evaluation for RECIST. The median time from diagnosis to start of nirogacestat was 89.2 months (range: 14.5 months to 223.3 months).
L1275: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1276: Demographic characteristics were consistent with the general population with DT.
L1277: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1278: The distribution of female to male was modestly higher than the distribution in the general population of patients with DT. The distribution of ages in this trial is reflective of the age distribution of patients with DT. This trial, like Study NIR-DT-301 and Study A8641014, enrolled mostly White patients (the remaining two patients were Black), and the proportion of non-White did not reflect the diversity of the U.S. population. The proportion of patients with CTNBB1 mutation (71%) is consistent with what is found in the general population with DT. Most patients had extra-abdominal disease (82%).
L1279: All the patients had previous therapy: systemic therapy (100%), surgery (76%), and radiotherapy (35%). Most patients received chemotherapy (82%), and the remainder of the patients received "drug and/or immunotherapy." Stable disease was the most common best response to previous therapy (76%), and one patient (6%) had a partial response to previous therapy.
L1280: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L1281: ## Data:
L1282: All 17 participants received at least one dose of nirogacestat. As of this data cut, the median duration of nirogacestat was 49.7 months (range: 2-82). The majority (10 [59%]) of participants were on nirogacestat for at least 3 years or longer, 2 participants (12%) were on nirogacestat for at least 1 year but less than 2 years and 5 participants (29%) were on nirogacestat for less than one year. The data are mutually exclusive and represent the longest time on study. The median number of cycles completed was 13 (range: 0 – 102). A cycle was considered completed if 90% of the planned dose was administered. The planned dose intensity was 300 mg/day. The median dose was 243 mg, less than the prescribed dose, which was not unexpected in a study of this duration given the number of dose modifications.
L1283: Fifteen participants had a total of 48 AEs leading to dose modifications. Four participants had 4 AEs that led to dose reductions (PTs included diarrhea, fatigue, memory impairment, and weight decreased). All 4 participants had a confirmed BOR of SD and 3 of the 4 had sustained response with a lower dose of nirogacestat. Participant [REDACTED](b) (6) experienced a &gt; 20% increase in tumor size from nadir and is shown as a PD in the derived response that occurred after the dose was reduced.
L1284: Fifteen participants had 37 AEs that led to drug interruptions, 1 participant had 1 AE that led to drug withdrawal (hypersensitivity), and 5 participants had 6 AEs that led to drug interruption
L1285: followed by dose reduction. The 5 participants with dose interruptions that led to dose reductions were also counted in the drug interruptions total but were not counted in the total for dose reductions. A total of 9 participants out of 17 (53%) had eventual dose reductions.
L1286: All participants received concomitant treatments during the study. The most commonly (&gt; 50%) reported ATC Class 3 of concomitant medications included: antipropulsives, antiemetics and antinauseants, antiinflammatory and antirheumatic products (non-steroids), other mineral supplements and opioids.
L1287: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1288: Nirogacestat was well tolerated with a median (range) duration of treatment of 49.7 months (2, 82). The most commonly reported concomitant medications were antipropulsives, antiemetics and antinauseants, antiinflammatory and antirheumatic products (non-steroids), other mineral supplements and opioids to manage diarrhea, nausea, and pain.
L1289: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1290: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of treatment adherence. The relative dose intensity was high (median 96%, range 0.8-263%; mean 86%, standard deviation 21%).
L1291: The AEs that led to dose interruption followed by dose reduction in the 5 patients were breast tenderness, QT prolongation, hypocalcemia, myalgia, and rash maculopapular (one patient each experienced these AEs except for rash, which was experienced by 2 patients). Only three AEs that led to a dose modification were experienced by more than 1 patient: rash maculopapular (n=3), hypophosphatemia (n=2), and nausea (n=2).
L1292: The most common indications for use of concomitant medications were pain (n=16, 94%), diarrhea (n=14, 82%), nausea and prophylaxis of nausea (n=13, 76%), hypophosphatemia (n=12, 71%), infection (n=10, 59%), rash (n=10, 59%), and supplement (n=9, 53%).
L1293: The most common indications for the use of concomitant medications in Study 14-C-0007 were similar to those of Study NIR-DT-301 and Study A8641014, and consistent with the effects of the tumor (i.e., pain) or with the adverse reactions associated with nirogacestat, such as diarrhea, nausea, hypophosphatemia, rash, and infection.
L1294: All the patients underwent concomitant procedures. Procedures that were performed on more than one patient that were not imaging or electrocardiograms were biopsy (n=6), incision and drainage (n=4), culture (n=2), and curative surgery (n=2; for non-DT reasons). Three patients underwent procedures due to DT (n=1 each for incision and drainage, surgery, and hysterectomy). Because Study 14-C-0007 was not a randomized trial and had a small sample size, the data from the concomitant procedure dataset did not contribute to our assessment to the safety and efficacy of nirogacestat.
L1295: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L1296: ## Data:
L1297: Among the 17 participants included in the study, there were 16 participants evaluable for response. One participant was not included in the efficacy analysis because the participant did not have any tumor measurements taken after starting nirogacestat. The ORR (95% CI) was 31% (11%-58.7%). Five participants achieved a confirmed BOR of PR and 10 participants achieved a confirmed BOR of SD by RECIST v1.1. One participant had tumor imaging performed post-dose and met the target lesion measurement criteria to be an SD; however, because tumor imaging was assessed only 3 weeks after starting study drug, this participant was considered not evaluable for BOR because a minimum of 4 weeks was required between scans to confirm a response.
L1298: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1299: Nirogacestat was effective at sustained disease stabilization, as shown by the ORR of 31%, SD of 63%, and PD of 0%.
L1300: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1301: FDA did not independently verify the results presented in this Assessment Aid by the Applicant for early phase trials, such as Study 14-C-0004, because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat for the treatment of patients with progressing DTs is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301.
L1302: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1303: ## Data:
L1304: All data were collected in a timely manner and reviewed by the investigator or a lead associate investigator. Any safety concerns, new information that affected either the ethical and or scientific conduct of the study, or protocol deviations and violations were immediately reported to the IRB using iRIS and to the sponsor.
L1305: The investigator reviewed AE and response data on each participant to ensure safety and data accuracy. The investigator conducted or supervised the investigation and provided appropriate delegation of responsibilities to other members of the research staff.
L1306: This study was monitored by personnel on contract to the NCI, NIH. Monitors were qualified by training and experience to monitor the progress of this clinical study. Personnel monitoring this study were not affiliated in any way with the study conduct.
L1307: At least 25% of enrolled participants were randomly selected and monitored at least biannually or as needed, based on accrual rate. The participants selected had 100% source document
L1308: verification done. Additional monitoring activities included: adherence to protocol specified study eligibility, treatment plans, data collection for safety and efficacy, reporting and time frames of AEs to the NCI IRB and FDA, and informed consent requirements. Written reports were generated in response to the monitoring activities and submitted to the investigator and Clinical Director or Deputy Clinical Director, Center for Cancer Research, NCI.
L1309: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1310: No issues were identified that could potentially impact data integrity, prevent an adequate assessment of the data, or change the conclusions drawn.
L1311: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1312: FDA agrees that Applicant has provided all the datasets and supporting metadata documentation required to validate the results.
L1313: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1314: ## **Data:**
L1315: ## **ORR by Mutation Type**
L1316: Of the 9 participants (53%) who had a CTNNB1 T41A tumor mutation, 3 had a BOR of PR and 5 had a BOR of SD; one additional participant was not evaluable for disease response. The ORR (95% CI) for the CTNNB1 T41A somatic mutation was 33% (7.5%-70.1%). The DCR (95% CI) was 89% (51.8%-99.7%).
L1317: Three participants had the CTNNB1 S45F tumor mutation, and all 3 had a BOR of SD. The ORR (95% CI) for the CTNNB1 S45F somatic mutation was 0% (0.0%-70.8%). The DCR (95% CI) was 100% (29.2%-100.0%).
L1318: Two participants had the APC A972G germline tumor mutation, and one participant had a BOR of PR. One participant was not evaluable as the participant did not have any tumor measurements taken. With only one evaluable participant, ORR and DCR were 100% (2.5%-100.0%)
L1319: The one participant that had the APC somatic tumor mutation had a BOR of SD. With only one evaluable participant, the ORR was 0% and the DCR was 100% (2.5%-100.0%)
L1320: Two participants did not have any mutation type and one participant had a BOR of PR and one had a BOR of SD. The ORR was 50% (1.3%-98.7%) and the DCR was 100% (15.8%-100.0%).
L1321: ## **DOR**
L1322: Among the 5 participants with a confirmed BOR of CR or PR, the median duration of response was not reached due to censoring (range: 41.1 – Not evaluable). One participant died before
L1323: progression and was censored at the time of death. Four participants were censored due to no documented disease progression (2 participants) and off study due to reasons other than disease progression (2 participants).
L1324: ## **Duration of Disease Control**
L1325: The DCR based on confirmed BOR (95% CI) was 94% (69.8%-99.8%).
L1326: Among the 15 participants with a confirmed BOR of CR, PR, or SD, the median duration of response was not reached due to censoring. One participant died before disease progression and one participant, with a BOR of SD, experienced a &gt; 20% increase in tumor size from nadir. Four participants were censored due to no documented progression or death and 9 participants went off study due to reasons other than disease progression.
L1327: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1328: Clinical benefit was observed regardless of mutational status of CTNNB1 or APC genes (Kummar et al. 2017). The DOR, DoSD, and DCR suggest that treatment with nirogacestat at 150 mg BID results in long-term disease stabilization and support the continued evaluation of nirogacestat for the treatment of DT.
L1329: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1330: FDA did not independently verify the results presented in this Assessment Aid by the Applicant for early phase trials, such as Study 14-C-0007, because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat for the treatment of patients with progressing DTs is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301. In addition, the sample size per type of mutation is too small in Study 14-C-0007 for FDA to draw any conclusions on effect of mutation on the efficacy of nirogacestat in patients with DT.
L1331: ## **Dose/Dose Response**
L1332: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1333: All participants were dosed at 150 mg BID; therefore, this is not applicable for this submission.
L1334: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1335: Nine patients underwent dose reduction, among whom 8 were evaluable for efficacy. Among the 8 evaluable-patients, one patient had a partial response, and the remaining patients had stable disease (n=6) or were not evaluable (n=1). All 9 patients who did not undergo dose reduction were evaluable for efficacy, among whom 4 patients had a partial response, and 4 patients had stable disease. The ORRs among patients who had dose reductions versus who did not were 12.5% (95% CI: 0.003, 0.527), and 50% (95% CI: 0.16, 0.84), respectively. The difference in ORR between these two subgroups suggests that there might be a dose-response
L1336: relationship for nirogacestat in patients with DT. However, the small sample size, and post-hoc nature of this subgroup analysis precludes the ability of the FDA to determine if there is a dose-response relationship based on Study 14-C-0007, since there could be confounders in this analysis.
L1337: ## **Durability of Response**
L1338: ## **Data:**
L1339: Median DOR for participants with ORs was not reached.
L1340: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1341: Response was durable among participants with ORs.
L1342: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1343: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position; however, FDA did not independently verify the results presented by the Applicant for Study 14-C-0007 because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301.
L1344: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1345: ## **Data:**
L1346: Refer to efficacy results – secondary and other relevant endpoints above.
L1347: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1348: The study has demonstrated persistence of effect over time.
L1349: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1350: FDA did not independently verify the results presented by the Applicant for Study 14-C-0007 because the evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat is based on the pivotal trial NIR-DT-301.
L1351: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory COA (PRO) Endpoints**
L1352: ## **Data:**
L1353: All participants were evaluable for PROs by using the MD Anderson Symptom Inventory. Results from this analysis were not centrally databased for inclusion in the CSR; however, they were published in Kummar et al. (2017). As described in this publication, partial responders had a 1.65-point improvement in mean symptom severity (p = 0.008). The five participants with stable disease as their best response had a 0.8-point improvement in mean symptom severity (p = 0.08). Of the 7 participants whose treatment was discontinued or who withdrew from the study, mean change in symptom severity was negligible.
L1354: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1355: Significant improvements in PROs were observed among participants receiving nirogacestat.
L1356: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1357: FDA limited its analysis of PROs to the results submitted from trial NIR-DT-301, as the design and sample size of Study 14-C-0007 precludes FDAs ability to meaningfully interpret these PRO results. See Section 8.1.1.2.
L1358: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1359: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1360: Not applicable.
L1361: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1362: Not applicable.
L1363: ## **8.1.10. Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1364: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1365: See Section 8.1.11.
L1366: ## **8.1.11. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials**
L1367: ## **Primary Endpoints**
L1368: ## Data
L1369: Integrated efficacy analyses are based on the Full Analysis Set (FAS). The FAS population consisted of all participants in the DB phase of Study NIR-DT-301 who were randomized to study treatment (nirogacestat or placebo) per the primary analysis data cut (07 April 2022), all participants enrolled in Study 14-C-0007 per a 20 November 2020 data cut, and the 2 participants from Study A8641014 with DT who received nirogacestat at the 150 mg BID dose level per a 09 January 2013 cutoff. The FAS population included 89 participants assigned to nirogacestat (1 participant did not receive nirogacestat) and 72 participants assigned to placebo.
L1370: In the Full Analysis Set (FAS) population, there was a statistically significant improvement in PFS in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm, with a 78% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death (HR = 0.22; 95% CI: 0.11, 0.42; p &lt; 0.001); this was consistent with the ITT population in Study NIR DT-301 (Section 8.1.1). Of the 13 (15%) participants in the nirogacestat
L1371: arm and 36 (50%) participants in the placebo arm who progressed, most were due to radiographic progression (11 [12%] participants and 30 [42%] participants, respectively). One participant in each arm died before PD.
L1372: The probability of being event-free at Months 12 and 24 was higher in the nirogacestat arm (88.0% and 82.2% respectively) than the placebo arm (52.8% and 44.0%, respectively) supporting a sustained clinical benefit over time.
L1373: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1374: Treatment with nirogacestat across studies resulted in a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in PFS compared with placebo.
L1375: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1376: Among the three trials used to support this NDA, only Study 14-C-0007 and Study NIR-DT-301 evaluated efficacy as a primary endpoint. The primary objective of the remaining trial, Study A8641014, was to determine the MTD and RP2D of nirogacestat for further development. Because Study NIR-DT-301 was the only randomized trial investigating nirogacestat, only the data from this trial can be used to evaluate PFS since time-to-event endpoints such as PFS cannot be evaluated in single-arm trials. FDA does not agree with pooling data across these three trials as they all had different research objectives and patients in the trials may have different characteristics. In addition, pooling data from non-randomized trials with data from randomized trials is not an appropriate approach as it breaks randomization such that patients' characteristics will be imbalanced between arms. This pooling approach will make results difficult to interpret as the treatment effect estimates will be unreliable. Thus, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's pooled analysis for PFS.
L1377: Study 14-C-0007 was a single-arm trial in 16-evaluable patients with DT, with a reported ORR of 31% (95% CI: 11, 59) and the median DoR was not reached (range: 41 months - NE).
L1378: Study NIR-DT-301 was a randomized trial in patients with DTs progressing based on RECIST 1.1, and it demonstrated a statistically and clinically significant improvement in the primary endpoint of PFS in patients treated with nirogacestat compared to placebo. The median PFS was not reached (95% CI: NE, NE) for patients on nirogacestat, and 15.1 months (95% CI: 8.4, NE) for patients on placebo, with a hazard ratio of 0.29 (95% CI: 0.15, 0.55; p-value &lt;0.001). This PFS included both clinical and radiographic progression as assessed by blinded independent review, and FDA agrees with the adjudication for clinical progression.
L1379: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1380: ## Data:
L1381: ## ORR
L1382: In the FAS population, the ORR was statistically significantly higher (p &lt; 0.001) in the nirogacestat arm (35 [40%] participants) versus the placebo arm (6 [8%] participants), including 5 (6%) participants with CR in the nirogacestat arm versus no (0%) participants in the placebo arm. Results in the FAS population were similar to the ITT population of Study NIR-DT-301.
L1383: ## DOR
L1384: Similar to the Study NIR-DT-301 ITT population, the Kaplan-Meier estimates of median DOR were not reached for either treatment arm (Messersmith et al. 2015; Kummar et al. 2017).
L1385: ## DCR
L1386: In the FAS population, the DCR was similar between the nirogacestat and placebo arms (93% and 85%; p = 0.088), respectively, which may have been due to the higher rate of SD in the placebo arm. The DCR was similar between the Study NIR-DT-301 ITT population and the FAS population.
L1387: ## DoSD
L1388: Similar to the NIR-DT-301 ITT population, the Kaplan-Meier estimates of DoSD were not reached for either treatment arm due to the low number of progressions or deaths in the FAS population.
L1389: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1390: Treatment with nirogacestat across studies resulted in a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in ORR compared with placebo. While data are limited, responses appear durable. Other efficacy analyses including change in tumor volume as assessed by volumetric MRI and change in T2 hypersensitivity and change in PRO measures demonstrate improvements with nirogacestat, further supporting the primary efficacy analysis.
L1391: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1392: FDA does not agree with Applicant's pooled analysis for endpoints including ORR, DOR, DCR, and DoSD. See FDA's comments above for Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials for the primary endpoint of PFS for more details regarding issues with pooling data across the three trials.
L1393: ## Subpopulations
L1394: ## Data
L1395: ## PFS
L1396: A statistically significant improvement in the primary endpoint of PFS in the nirogacestat arm compared with the placebo arm (FAS population) was observed across most demographic; disease characteristic; prior treatment; and genetic mutation, dose intensity, and AE subgroups. While the upper boundary of the CIs for a few subgroups were &gt; 1, in all instances this was due to a small sample size.
L1397: ## ORR
L1398: The statistically significant improvement in ORR for nirogacestat over placebo with the FAS population was maintained across most demographic, disease characteristics, prior treatments, and genetic mutation, dose intensity, and AE subgroups.
L1399: ## DCR
L1400: In the FAS population, the DCR was similar between the nirogacestat and placebo arms across most subgroups. Statistically significant improvements in DCR in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm were observed in participants with baseline tumor size \ge 89.575 to &lt; 140.35 mm (p = 0.033), no history of FAP (p = 0.019), and participants who did not receive prior radiation therapy (p = 0.049).
L1401: ## DoSD
L1402: Similar to the overall population, no conclusions regarding DoSD evaluated by subgroup can be drawn due to the low number responses and subsequent events observed.
L1403: ## Exposure
L1404: Exposure was evaluated by subgroup; no notable differences between treatment arms were observed.
L1405: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1406: Results of subgroup analyses were consistent across studies, with statistically significant improvements in PFS and ORR observed across most subgroups as compared to placebo.
L1407: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1408: FDA does not agree with Applicant's presentation of subgroup analyses based on the pooled data. See FDA's comments above for Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials for the primary endpoint of PFS for more details regarding issues with pooling data across the three trials.
L1409: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1410: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1411: OS was not evaluated in the three trials submitted to support this NDA. FDA did not object to this plan because DT is rarely fatal and the existing safety data for nirogacestat indicated that it is not expected to lead to safety-related deaths.
L1412: ## 8.1.12. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1413: ## Data:
L1414: Overall, analyses performed on the FAS population for the integrated summary of efficacy yielded similar results to analyses of the ITT population performed for the pivotal NIR-DT-301 Study.
L1415: - In the FAS population, participant disposition, demographics, and baseline characteristics in the nirogacestat arm were similar to those in the placebo group. The nirogacestat arm had a lower proportion of participants with events meeting the study endpoint than the placebo arm (14% vs 49%, respectively) and a higher proportion of participants in the nirogacestat arm than in the placebo arm were ongoing at the time of the data cut (48% vs 32%, respectively).
- In the FAS population, there was a statistically significant improvement in PFS in the nirogacestat arm versus the placebo arm, with a 78% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death (HR = 0.22; 95% CI: 0.12, 0.43; p &lt; 0.001); this was consistent with the ITT population from Study NIR-DT-301. The ORR was significantly higher (p &lt; 0.001) in the nirogacestat arm (34 [40%] participants) versus the placebo arm (6 [8%] participants), including 5 (6%) participants with CR in the nirogacestat arm versus no (0%) participants in the placebo arm.
L1416: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1417: In summary, treatment with nirogacestat resulted in statistically significant improvements in PFS, ORR, and patient-reported symptoms and impacts, demonstrating a clear and consistent benefit in participants receiving nirogacestat as compared to placebo. The durability of the PFS advantage over a time scale of up to 7 years, combined with substantial reduction in disease progression, increase in response rate, and significant improvements in pain, DT symptom burden, physical functioning, role functioning, and HRQoL, support a clinically meaningful benefit conferred by nirogacestat. Ultimately, these data support the efficacy of nirogacestat in the treatment of DT.
L1418: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1419: FDA does not agree with the analyses performed using pooled data as described by the Applicant in the integrated summary of efficacy. See FDA's comments above for Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials for the primary endpoint of PFS for more details regarding issues with pooling data across the three trials. However, FDA agrees that in the pivotal Study NIR-DT-301, nirogacestat led to an improvement in PFS, and ORR that was durable and consistent across subgroups, and pain improvement in those who experienced \ge 2 BPI at baseline in patients with DT.
L1420: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1421: ## Data:
L1422: Refer to Section 8.2.4 for summaries of deaths, AEs, SAEs, AEs leading to discontinuations, and other safety data.
L1423: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1424: Nirogacestat had a manageable and acceptable safety profile in participants with DT.
L1425: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1426: In general, FDA agrees with the above statement; however, refer to Section 1 above and the safety review below regarding additional considerations. For example, the FDA review team recommended revising the indication statement to specify patients have progressing DT who need systemic treatment as select patients may be appropriate for a watch-and-wait approach or surgery. Given that nirogacestat can cause severe toxicities including diarrhea and given the potential for ovarian toxicity and hepatotoxicity, patients will need close monitoring as described in labeling and patients should be adequately consented (standard practice in oncology) for the safe and effective use of nirogacestat.
L1427: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1428: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1429: The data pooling strategy for this SCS was agreed upon by the FDA at the Pre-NDA meeting. The studies are grouped into the following study pools depending on the study design, participants, dosing regimen, and targeted underlying conditions:
L1430: - All DT Pool (All DT Pool) to inform labeling (Studies NIR-DT-301, 14-C-0007, and A8641014 [participants with DT only])
- All Cancer Participants Treated with Monotherapy (All CM Pool) (All DT Pool, all participants in A8641014, and Study A8641020)
L1431: - HVs Treated with Single Dose (HV Pool) (Studies A8641001, A8641008, NIR-DT-101, NIR-DT-102 and NIR-DT-103)
L1432: The studies in the All DT Pool and All CM Pool were pooled to characterize the overall safety of nirogacestat monotherapy in participants with DT and solid tumors including DT, respectively. Five single dose HV studies (HV Pool), were summarized by active drug and by placebo or other comparator. In addition, given that the OLE phase of Study NIR-DT-301 is still ongoing and the data are considered interim, descriptive statistics from this phase of the study were provided; however, this phase of the study was not pooled with other study data and is presented separately in select outputs (OLE Group).
L1433: Data from 8 participants with T-ALL/LBL in the A8641014 study and 3 completed studies (A8641002, A8641016, and A8641019) with nirogacestat were excluded from the data pooling for this summary of safety. As agreed upon at the Pre-NDA meeting, participants with T-ALL/LBL were excluded from the integrated safety analyses as these hematologic malignancies were anticipated to be sufficiently different from DT to preclude any comparisons regarding safety data. In addition, given that Study A8641002 was the only study evaluating multi-dose administration of nirogacestat in a HV participant population, these data were summarized separately from the single dose HV studies.
L1434: Note that there is overlap of the populations of the nirogacestat pools analyzed. The focus of this Assessment Aid will be on the All DT Pool, with comparisons to the NIR-DT-301 data since this is the only study that includes a placebo comparator arm.
L1435: The All DT Pool included 141 participants from the DB phase of Study NIR-DT-301 (69 participants exposed to nirogacestat and 72 participants exposed to placebo), 17 participants from Study 14-C-0007, and 9 participants from Study A8641014.
L1436: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1437: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the safety population included in the primary safety analyses. FDA clarifies that the FDA primary safety analysis was performed on patients who were enrolled to NIR-DT-301 and received at least one dose of study treatment (N= 141) including 69 patients who received nirogacestat and 72 patients who received placebo in the double-blind portion of the trial. Verbatim descriptions of AEs were mapped to the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 24.0 for AEs. The severity of AEs and laboratory abnormalities were graded according to CTCAE v5.0. The safety monitoring period was from the time informed consent was signed until 30 days after the last treatment dose. The data cutoff for the primary safety analysis was April 7, 2022. A safety update was provided on March 27, 2023, which consisted of data from the final database lock (June 30, 2022) for NIR-DT-301 double-blind portion and data for the ongoing NIR-DT-301 open-label extension (OLE) portion (October 24, 2022, interim data cut) and ongoing Study 14-C-0007
L1438: (December 1, 2022, interim safety data cut). The data was reviewed for congruence with the primary safety analysis.
L1439: Although the primary safety analysis focused on the results of Study NIR-DT-301, and these results are described in product labeling with agreed upon group terms, the review of safety in certain sections below (for example, deaths and SAEs) also included an assessment of the broader safety pools.
L1440: The FDA safety review focused on the incidence of TEAEs regardless of causality assessment. The safety analysis including fatal and non-fatal SAEs, AEs leading to permanent treatment discontinuation, treatment interruption and treatment delay.
L1441: FDA analysis utilized clinically relevant grouped terms independent of the analyses provided by the Applicant. Therefore, there may be some discrepancies between the results for the safety table presented in his section.
L1442: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1443: ## Overall Exposure
L1444: ## Data:
L1445: Key aspects of exposure and compliance for participants receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID are shown in the table below. The mean duration of exposure for the nirogacestat 150 mg BID group was similar in Study NIR-DT-301 and the All CM Pool, and longer in the All DT Pool. While the median duration of exposure for the 150 mg dose group was similar for Study NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool, it was shorter in the All CM Pool. The relative dose intensity for nirogacestat 150 mg BID was similar across Study NIR-DT-301, the All DT, and All CM Pools for both the mean and median values.

L1450: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1451: The overall exposure to nirogacestat is adequate to support characterization of the safety profile.
L1452: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1453: A total of 69 patients received at least one dose of nirogacestat in NIR-DT-301. The median exposure of approximately 21 months in Study NIR-DT-301 in patients randomized to nirogacestat provided for a reasonable assessment of the common toxicities related to the chronic use of this drug, particularly considering the rarity of the intended population. The duration of exposure may not be adequate to fully characterize long term ovarian toxicity (refer to review below and consult from Division of Urology, Obstetrics, and Gynecology [DUOG]).
L1454: ## **Relevant Characteristics of the Safety Population:**
L1455: ## Data:
L1456: The demographics and other characteristics of the groups receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID in the All DT, All CM Pool, and OLE Pools are shown in Table 50. In general, the participants in Study NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool were representative of the DT population, with a female predominance of 2-3 fold (Reitamo et al. 1982; de Camargo et al. 2010; Skubitz 2017; Anneberg et al. 2022).
L1457: Overall, the demographic characteristics in the All DT Pool were similar between treatment groups, considering the small number of participants in the nirogacestat treatment groups other than 150 mg BID. The dose groups other than 150 mg BID included no participants ≥ 46 years of age, or from parts of the world other than North America.
