L1464: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1465: In general, the participants in Study NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool were representative of the DT population, with a female predominance of 2 to 3-fold. Participants in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID group of All CM Pool were older than the participants in NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool, which was expected given the different indications under study and the fact that DT typically occurs in a younger population as compared to the other solid tumors evaluated.
L1466: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1467: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment regarding the primary safety analysis. FDA clarifies that the FDA primary safety analysis was performed on patients who were enrolled to NIR-DT-301 and received at least one dose of study treatment (N= 141) including 69 receiving nirogacestat and 72 receiving placebo in the double-blind portion of the trial.
L1468: With respect to applicability of the Study NIR-DT-301 population to the US population, the median age (33.5 years in nirogacestat-treated patients), increased prevalence in women, appears consistent with DT in the US. There were insufficient numbers of patients to assess treatment effects among non-White patients and among older patients with DT.
L1469: ## Adequacy of the Safety Database:
L1470: ## Data
L1471: Key aspects of exposure and compliance for participants receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID are shown in Table 49.
L1472: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1473: The evaluation of safety is based on data from Study NIR-DT-301 and the nirogacestat 150 mg BID dose level in the All DT Pool. These populations allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of nirogacestat 150 mg BID and the overall risk-benefit in patients with DT.
L1474: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1475: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of exposure and compliance for patients receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID. FDA clarifies that the FDA primary safety analysis was performed on patients who were enrolled to NIR-DT-301 which provides an informed assessment of the safety profile of nirogacestat 150 mg BID in patients with DT. The size and exposure of the overall safety data set is reasonable in this rare patient population to provide an estimate of common adverse reactions over time.
L1476: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1477: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1478: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1479: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections; data in the CRFs and AE databases were consistent.
L1480: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1481: During review of the Application, the OSI audit noted that the Applicant provided corrected and updated datasets that affected up to 16 tables and six figures for laboratory summary and shift tables, and one table for concomitant medications. These updated tables and figures were received on May 5, 2023. In addition, incomplete information to assess the PRO data which led to Information Requests for compliance and completion rates, and updated datasets were received from the Applicant on April 21, 2023, May 5, 2023, May 30, 2023, and on June 7, 2023. Refer to Section 4 of this review (Office of Scientific Investigation) for further details.
L1482: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1483: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1484: Safety analyses included drug exposure and compliance, AEs, clinical laboratory and electrocardiogram (ECG) parameters, ECOG Performance Status, and vital signs.
L1485: All AEs were coded using the MedDRA coding system (MedDRA Version 24) and displayed in tables and data listings using SOC and PT. Analyses of AEs were performed for those events that were considered treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), where treatment-emergent is defined per protocol as any AE with initial onset or increasing in severity after the first dose of study treatment through 30 days after the last dose of study treatment. To facilitate a better understanding of specific topics of interest that may have comprised multiple PTs, select TEAEs were evaluated by pre-defined categories.
L1486: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1487: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's categorization of adverse events. The Applicant provided appropriate definitions and reporting requirements for AEs and serious AEs (SAEs). AE evaluation was performed at the time of informed consent up to 30 days after the last dose of study treatment. In the ADAE dataset, there were 2912 individual listed AEs of which 128 were not treatment emergent (88 from the primary treatment phase and 40 from the open-label extension phase). Of the 88 AEs in the primary treatment phase, all but 29 occurred prior to the start date. Of these 29, 9 events [7 nirogacestat and 2 placebo] did not have a start date and the rest occurred 37 or more days after the start date. None of the 9 events without a start date were serious and no AE term among these 9 occurred more than once. Overall the TEAE definition used by the sponsor appeared acceptable.
L1488: During the course of Study NIR-DT-301, Patients reporting AEs/AESIs/SAEs associated with ovarian dysfunction (amenorrhea, ovarian failure, or premature menopause) had hormone levels assessed every 3 months until event resolution (or for at least 90 days after discontinuing study treatment) since Amendment 3 dated January 27, 2020. FDA analyses were conducted irrespective of attribution and group terms were used that may be different than what is in the final USPI for nirogacestat.
L1489: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1490: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1491: Laboratory, ECGs, and vital signs assessments were performed according to the Schedule of Activities in each protocol. Available data were summarized using the Safety population from all the study pools as well as the OLE Phase of Study NIR-DT-301. Laboratory results were not available for Study 14-C-0007.
L1492: Clinical laboratory values were expressed in conventional units. Normal ranges were determined based on the ranges supplied by the laboratory and were not normalized. Missing normal ranges were replaced by ranges from the central laboratory from the same study or standard laboratory ranges.
L1493: If ECG results were collected in triplicates, the average of the triplicate ECG records was calculated and used as analysis values for each visit. Additionally, for the All CM Pool and All DT Pool, the most extreme value, and its change from baseline per Cycle (1, 2, 3, 4 - 6, 7-12 and 13 and onward) was summarized. The most extreme ECGs were defined as the highest post-baseline value for QT, QTcF, QRS and PR intervals and the largest absolute change for heart rate.
L1494: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1495: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement. FDA further notes blood chemistries, hematology assessments were obtained at baseline, Cycle 1 Days 1, 8, 15, and 22, Cycle 2 day 28, Cycle 4 Day 1 and every third cycle thereafter on Day 1. Pregnancy tests were conducted in WOCBP at baseline, prior to first dose of study treatment, on Cycle 1 Day 22, Cycle 2 Day 28, then monthly. With institution of amendment #3 (January 27, 2020) menstrual history and female and male hormone levels were collected. Female hormones (TSH, Prolactin, AMH, LH, FSH, estradiol, and progesterone) were collected at baseline, Cycle 1 Day 22, Cycle 2 Day 28, Cycle 4 Day 1 and every 3 cycles thereafter. Male hormones (total testosterone, free testosterone, progesterone FSH, and LH) were collected prior to first treatment of study drug, Cycle 4 Day 1 and every 3 cycles thereafter.
L1496: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1497: ## Deaths
L1498: ## Data:
L1499: A total of 3 deaths were reported in the All DT Pool and all were assessed by the investigator as unrelated to nirogacestat. The incidence of TEAEs that led to death was the same in both the nirogacestat and placebo treatment groups. In Study NIR-DT-301, one participant in the placebo arm died due to sepsis. One participant in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID arm of Study NIR-DT-301 died due to multi-organ failure more than 30 days after discontinuing study treatment in the setting of aggressive tumor progression and a new diagnosis of spindle cell sarcoma. One participant from Study 14-C-0007 died from a cerebrovascular accident.
L1500: All other TEAEs with fatal outcome were reported from participants being treated for advanced cancer in the All CM Pool. In this pool, there were 7 deaths (4%) across all dosing groups, including 3 deaths (2%) in the nirogacestat 150 mg arm. Five of the deaths in this pool were
L1501: attributed to disease progression; the other 2 reported deaths were due to other causes in Studies 14-C-0007 and A8541020. The increase in number of deaths in the All CM Pool compared to the All DT Pool was driven by the older and sicker population of participants in these early studies of participants with advanced cancer.
L1502: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1503: Nirogacestat was not considered as a cause of death for any reported event. TEAEs leading to death were consistent with the underlying disease or disease progression.
L1504: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1505: At the time of the NDA submission, one patient who received nirogacestat died in Study NIR-DT-301; one death was also reported in the placebo arm. The patient who died in the nirogacestat arm in Study NIR-DT-301 had an identified APC mutation and was diagnosed with spindle cell sarcoma which had previously been reported as a pulmonary nodule. The patient discontinued nirogacestat on Day 65 and subsequently died on Day 108 of multiorgan failure. FDA agrees with the investigator (and Applicant) that this patient's death is unlikely related to nirogacestat.
L1506: One additional patient with desmoid tumor died in NCI trial 14-C-0007. This patient was 60-years-old person who died of a cerebrovascular accident with a CT-confirmed pontine hemorrhage. The patient had atrial fibrillation in the hospital and the investigator deemed the event as unlikely related. This attribution appears reasonable noting that the patient was on his sixth year of treatment and the patient also had severe hypertension as a potential contributing factor for the CVA.
L1507: Other deaths reported in nirogacestat development occurred in patients with advanced malignancies. All but two were attributed in the application to underlying disease or disease progression. One of the two patients died of thrombosis prior to randomization and one patient with advanced TNBC and ischemic heart disease experienced dyspnea and died. The investigator deemed the event as unrelated; however, additional details were lacking for this patient; therefore there is uncertainty regarding this patient's cause of death.
L1508: No new deaths were reported at the time of the 120-day safety update.
L1509: ## **Serious Adverse Events**
L1510: ## Data:
L1511: In the All DT Pool, SAEs were reported in 23 participants (26%) in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID arm. In the nirogacestat 150 mg BID arm, premature menopause (3 participants) and hematuria (2 participants) were the only PTs reported in more than 1 participant; in the placebo arm; sepsis (3 participants) and COVID-19 (2 participants) were reported in more than 1 participant.
L1512: All 3 reports of premature menopause were reported in Study NIR-DT-301 as an unexpected event early in the study (prior to identification of OD as an adverse event of special interest [AESI]) and were considered serious due to medical importance; more information is provided in Section 8.2.5. Overall, the findings in the All DT Pool align with those described in Study NIR-DT-301.
L1513: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1514: The events and rates of SAEs were generally consistent across studies and are representative of the overall safety profile. The only SAE reported by &gt; 2 participants receiving nirogacestat was premature menopause. The relatively low incidence of SAEs supports a favorable benefit:risk profile with nirogacestat in the treatment of patients with DT.
L1515: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1516: In Study NIR-DT-301 (in the double-blind phase), SAEs were reported in 20% of patients in the nirogacestat arm versus 11% among patients in the placebo arm. Overall SAEs of infections were similar between arms (6% for nirogacestat versus 7% for placebo) and were therefore, not included in product labeling. The only organ system with a difference between arms was for ovarian toxicity (4% versus 0% among placebo) (*note that the group term of ovarian toxicity differs from the more general term of reproductive system disorders described in the original application*). Among the 4% of patients with ovarian toxicity SAEs, there were three patients with an SAE of premature menopause and one patient with reported ovarian failure.
L1517: In Study NCI-14-C-0007, following an update to the safety database, there were two SAEs considered by the investigators as possibly related to nirogacestat: squamous cell carcinoma and vulvovaginal inflammation. These were Grade 2 in severity. No SAE occurred in more than one patient. The labeling for the nirogacestat includes a Warning for non-melanoma skin cancers (in NIR-DT-301, cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma and basal cell carcinoma occurred in 2.9% and 1.4% of patients, respectively).
L1518: When considering the combined DT pool of patients, the only terms that occurred in more than one patient were premature menopause (n=3) and hematuria (n=2).
L1519: The safety update described additional SAEs in the placebo to nirogacestat group in NIR-DT-301. One patient experienced an SAE of proctalgia and one of pyelonephritis. Both events were considered by the investigator/sponsor as unrelated to nirogacestat. One additional patient in the safety update was diagnosed with Grade 3 intestinal fistula and intestinal abscess. This patient with FAP had a prior history of pelvic abscesses and fistulas and these events were considered unrelated to nirogacestat.
L1520: In summary, although there is a slight increase in SAEs among patients who received nirogacestat versus placebo in Study NIR-DT-301 (double-blind phase), the major explanation
L1521: for this difference was ovarian toxicity (also, exposure was longer in the nirogacestat arm). In general, it is difficult to ascribe most of the specific SAEs to nirogacestat due to low frequency of individual events (however, non melanoma skin cancers, an event observed in the NCI study, is described in product labeling).
L1522: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1523: ## Data:
L1524: Events leading to treatment discontinuation that occurred in &gt; 1 participant in the All DT Pool were consistent with what was observed in Study NIR-DT-301. In the All DT Pool, 17 participants (19%) treated with nirogacestat 150 mg BID discontinued study treatment compared to 2 participants (3%) treated with placebo (dysphagia and drug-induced liver injury). The most frequently reported PTs (occurring in &gt; 1 participant in the nirogacestat 150 mg arm) were: diarrhea (4 participants), ALT increased (3 participants), premature menopause (3 participants), and AST increased (2 participants).
L1525: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1526: The profile of TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation was consistent with the known safety profile of nirogacestat and GSIs.
L1527: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1528: In the DeFi study, per the AE dataset, 16 of 69 patients (23%) who received nirogacestat in the DB phase discontinued due to an AE vs 2 (3%) in patients who discontinued placebo. Per the CSR, two of these 16 patients in the nirogacestat arm discontinued due to disease progression with a concomitant AE occurring at the time of treatment discontinuation (such that 14 patients or 20% discontinued due to a nirogacestat AE, as described in product labeling). The AE for one of the two patients was tumor hemorrhage. The AE for the other patient was hematuria occurring in a patient with a tumor located in the pelvis with vital structure involvement, including bladder, kidney, and intestinal tract. This patient's narrative is discussed in the review above (clinical progression event)
L1529: The most common PTs occurring in more than one patient in the nirogacestat arm were those listed by the Applicant: diarrhea (4 participants), ALT increased (3 participants), premature menopause (3 participants), and AST increased (2 participants). One of the 4 patients who discontinued nirogacestat with diarrhea also had hot flushes at the time of discontinuation. One additional patient discontinued nirogacestat due to ovarian failure.
L1530: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1531: ## Data:
L1532: For the purposes of this Assessment Aid, Grade \ge 3 TEAEs are considered significant.
L1533: TEAEs of Grade \ge 3 in the All DT Pool occurred in 60% of participants treated with nirogacestat 150 mg and comprised of 5% of total TEAE events (130/2543 events). Fifty participants (57%) reported Grade 3 events, 2 participants reported Grade 4 events (spindle cell sarcoma and appendicitis), and 1 participant reported a death (cerebrovascular accident). The most frequently reported Grade 3 TEAEs across all groups were diarrhea (15 participants; 16%) and hypophosphatemia (15 participants; 16%). For the nirogacestat 150 mg BID group in the All DT Pool, the most frequently reported Grade 3 TEAEs were diarrhea (14 participants [16%]) and hypophosphatemia (11 participants [13%]). Grade 3 hypophosphatemia was notably higher in the All DT Pool 150 mg BID group compared to Study NIR-DT-301 (11 participants [13%] vs 2 participants [3%]) (See Table below).
L1534: The incidences of TEAEs of Grade 3 occurring in the groups receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID in the All DT, All CM Pool, and OLE Pools were generally aligned with the nirogacestat 150 mg BID arm in Study NIR-DT-301.

L1538: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1539: Ninety-five percent of all reported TEAEs were Grade 1 or 2. Overall, the incidence of Grade 3 TEAEs was relatively low and only 2 Grade 4 events reported among participants with DT at any dose level; neither of these events were considered related to nirogacestat. The most frequently reported Grade 3 TEAE was diarrhea; Grade 3 hypophosphatemia was also frequently reported in the All DT Pool. These observations were effectively managed with
L1540: appropriate interventions and are consistent with the safety profile of GSIs supporting a favorable benefit:risk profile.
L1541: **The FDA's Assessment:** FDA would generally not agree that the incidence of Grade 3 TEAEs was low noting that 55% of patients treated with nirogacestat in DeFi were described as having a Grade 3 or greater TEAEs vs 17% for placebo. Nevertheless, most of the severe toxicities are those that can be managed or mitigated by supportive care or dose modifications. For example, the most common clinical Grade 3 AE was diarrhea. Skin toxicities such as folliculitis and rash also occurred in 6% of patients each.
L1542: DeFi used CTCAE version 5 to assess severity of AEs which potentially resulted in a lower incidence of Grade 3 hypophosphatemia because CTCAE v5 requires clinical sequelae for Grade 3. Nevertheless, 20% of patients in DeFi experienced phosphate levels less than 2 mg/dL (similar to criteria in CTCAE version 4).
L1543: With respect to Grade 4 events occurring in DeFi, there were three listed patients in the ADAE dataset. One patient was described above in this review (diagnosis of spindle cell sarcoma) and one patient was diagnosed with appendicitis. One additional patient was listed as having Grade 4 obesity with a start date one day after switching from placebo to OLE (as such would not consider this as related to treatment with nirogacestat).
L1544: Refer to sections below (TEAEs and Application-Specific Safety Issues) for further exploration of TEAEs of all grades (including Grade 3 or above).

Overall, the most frequently (&gt; 30%) reported TEAEs in the NIR-DT-301 study and All DT Pool were diarrhea, nausea, fatigue, hypophosphatemia, and rash maculopapular, which are expected with GS inhibition (Krop et al. 2012; Tolcher et al. 2012; Richter et al. 2014; Messersmith et al. 2015; Pant et al. 2016).

L1561: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1562: The most frequently reported AEs (&gt; 20% in Study NIR-DT-301) were diarrhea, nausea, fatigue, hypophosphatemia, rash maculopapular, stomatitis headache, dermatitis acneiform, and vomiting (with vomiting being reported with a similar incidence in the placebo arm). OD occurred in 75% of WOCBP. Additional frequently reported AEs in the All DT Pool include AST increased, hot flush, ALT increased, abdominal pain, and cough. These were generally consistent with the effects of GS inhibition. The majority of participants (97%) reported their first TEAE in Cycle 1, and 95% of all reported TEAEs were Grade &lt; 3.
L1563: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1564: In general, FDA was able to reproduce the incidence rate of individual adverse events in Study NIR-DT-301 described in Table 52 above using standard FDA-run analyses/scripts (of SDTM datasets) with the exception of nausea (FDA script 55% for nirogacestat, headache 30% nirogacestat, and ALT increased 17% for nirogacestat). With alternative (but similar) methodology, grouping TEAEs in the dataset by patient ID, organ class, preferred term (by max toxicity grade), and arm, FDA was able to replicate the applicant's results for these three AE terms.
L1565: Although there were potentially relevant adverse terms below the 20% AE threshold described in the heading of Table 52, (b) (4) or inclusion as a grouped term, for example, as shown in Table 53 for rash, fatigue, and ovarian dysfunction.

L1577: ## Laboratory Findings
L1578: Data from physical examinations, ECOG scores, vital signs, and laboratory safety evaluations were not centrally databased for Study 14-C-0007 and were therefore not included in the integrated analyses of these data.
L1579: ## Data:
L1580: ## Hematology
L1581: In the All DT Pool and All CM Pools, an increase from baseline in basophils and eosinophils was observed by the first cycle and persisted into the third cycle. Concurrently, the percentage of basophils and eosinophils out of total leukocytes also increased from baseline.
L1582: In Study NIR-DT-301, post-treatment eosinophil values consistently trended higher among participants receiving nirogacestat as compared to placebo; this finding did not appear to correlate with an increased rate of AEs of eosinophilia or AEs associated with the development of allergic reactions. A persistent increase in the basophil/leukocyte and eosinophil/leukocyte ratios, along with a transient small decrease in absolute lymphocyte count, is suggestive of a mild inflammatory state associated with on-target activity of nirogacestat.
L1583: Laboratory data from clinical studies do not suggest that nirogacestat 150 mg BID is associated with changes in lymphocyte or neutrophil count values that are different from those observed in the placebo arm of Study NIR-DT-301. Participants from studies other than NIR-DT-301 in the All DT Pool did not contribute meaningful data to inform this aspect of the safety profile of
L1584: nirogacestat. Observations from the data in the All CM Pool and OLE Group were aligned with those from Study NIR-DT-301.
L1585: ## Clinical Chemistry
L1586: There was a trend toward increased ALT and AST values and decreased phosphate and potassium in the All DT Pool and All CM Pool compared to placebo. Alkaline phosphatase, glucose, total bilirubin, albumin, total protein, creatinine, and chloride remained relatively stable in both treatment arms.
L1587: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1588: A persistent increase in the basophil/leukocyte and eosinophil/leukocyte ratio along with a transient small decrease in absolute lymphocyte count is suggestive of a mild inflammatory state associated with on-target activity of nirogacestat. The data described above concerning the effect of nirogacestat on lymphocytes and neutrophils do not suggest that GS inhibition due to nirogacestat is having adverse effect on their numbers, and the data are not consistent with an immunosuppressed state.
L1589: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1590: In general, the most important (clinically) laboratory abnormalities appeared to be increases in liver enzymes and depletion of electrolytes. Because laboratory data generally describe the finding more accurately than AE data, FDA recommended inclusion of a separate laboratory finding table in product labeling. Overall, the incidence of hyperbilirubinemia was slightly higher in the placebo arm (11% versus 6%) with one Grade 3 or greater event in both arms. No case of Hy's law was observed in nirogacestat-treated patients in Study NIR-DT-301.
L1591: FDA recommend inclusion of phosphate levels &lt; 2 mg/dL in Section 5 of labeling in patients treated with nirogacestat (20%) because below 2 mg/dL represents hypophosphatemia that should be acted upon to prevent development of symptomatic disease.

L1597: ## Urinalysis
L1598: ## Data
L1599: There was an increased incidence of Grade 1 proteinuria and Grade 1 glucosuria in the nirogacestat arm of Study NIR-DT-301 compared to the placebo arm.
L1600: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1601: The observation of an increased incidence of Grade 1 proteinuria and glucosuria in participants receiving nirogacestat compared to placebo, along with a plausible effect of GS inhibition on the functioning of renal tubule epithelium, supports recognizing proteinuria and glycosuria as identified risks for nirogacestat.
L1602: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1603: Overall and as described in CSR, more patients in the nirogacestat arm with no Grade proteinuria at Baseline showed Grade 1 at the end of cycle 2 (27% versus 12%); however, there was no difference at the beginning of cycle 4 and no reported events that were Grade 2 or greater. There was a small difference in rate of Grade 1 proteinuria at Cycle 10 (20% versus 12%) as well as a difference in glycosuria between arms (b) (4)
L1604: ## Vital Signs
L1605: ## Data:
L1606: No clinically significant trends were identified in vital sign parameters when compared to placebo or across dose subgroups.
L1607: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1608: Nirogacestat did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on participant vital signs during any study.
L1609: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1610: FDA agrees that there did not appear to be sustained clinically significant changes in blood pressure or heart rate upon review of box-whisker plots in the application.
L1611: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1612: ## Data:
L1613: No clinically significant trends were identified in ECG parameters. In the All DT Pool, 9 participants (10%) reported QTcF increases of 30-60 msec. This was increased compared to placebo (1 participant [1%]) and was consistent across subgroups of age, gender, BMI, race, dose intensity, and prior therapy. No increases in QTcF &gt; 60 msec were identified in the All DT Pool nirogacestat 150 mg arm.
L1614: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1615: Nirogacestat did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on ECG values during any study.
L1616: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1617: See below for QT assessment. Per the QT-IRT review, there were no patients who experienced PR values &gt; 220 msec with 25% increase over baseline or QRS value &gt; 120 msec with 25% increase over baseline in Study NIR-DT-103 or in Study A8641014.
L1618: ## QT
L1619: A concentration-QT (C-QT) analysis was conducted using time-matched PK and triplicate 12-lead ECG data from five Phase 1 studies in healthy participant studies (A8641001, A8641002, NIR-DT-101, NIR-DT-102, and NIR-DT-103) and 2 studies in participants with DT (NIR-DT-301) and advanced solid tumors or T-ALL/LBL (A8641014). The final C-QT model was used to predict the nirogacestat concentration-related change in QTcF in participants with DT and in participants with other advanced solid tumors or T-ALL/LBL. For participants with DT, the mean
L1620: predicted \DeltaQTcF and 90% CI were below 10 msec for each evaluated concentration of interest, including 2 \times C_{max} at steady-state in the presence of strong CYP3A4 inhibition. For participants with other advanced malignancies, as evaluated in Study A8641014, the mean predicted \DeltaQTcF and 90% CI were below 10 msec for each evaluated concentration of interest, including 2 \times C_{max} at steady-state in the presence of moderate CYP3A4 inhibition.
L1621: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1622: The results of a C-QT analysis do not support the existence of a clinically significant prolongation of QTcF intervals associated with therapeutic dosing of nirogacestat alone or with moderate CYP3A4 inhibition.
L1623: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1624: Although the maximum change in QT from baseline was slightly higher in the nirogacestat arm IN Study NIR-DT-301 (12% vs 1% with a 30 msec increase or greater, per the applicant's CSR), a detailed review by via FDA QT-IRT determined that nirogacestat does not cause large mean increases in mean QTc interval (i.e., 20 msec or more) at the approved dose. There were no subjects in Study NIR-DT-103 who experienced a QTcF of greater than 450 msec.
L1625: ## Immunogenicity
L1626: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1627: Nirogacestat is a small molecule and not expected to elicit an immune response.
L1628: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1629: FDA agrees.
L1630: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1631: In Study NIR-DT-301, designation of an event as an AESI was an investigator decision based on guidance provided in the protocol. According to the protocol, most AESI PTs (except drug reactions) had to be Grade \ge 2 to qualify for designation as an AESI. AESIs were not specified in other studies included in this summary.
L1632: To better characterize AESIs and to account for legacy studies in which no AESIs were prospectively identified per the protocol, AESIs for the integrated safety analyses were identified by reported PT and included the following topics: OD; skin events of rash, hidradenitis suppurativa, and folliculitis; electrolyte insufficiency events of hypophosphatemia and hypokalemia; elevated liver enzymes; and hypersensitivity drug reactions. Of note, events of Grade \ge 3 hypomagnesemia were listed as AESIs in the NIR-DT-301 protocol; however, no Grade \ge 3 events of hypomagnesemia were reported in the NIR-DT-301 study or in the All DT, All CM, or OLE Pools at any dose level.
L1633: ## Data:
L1634: ## **Ovarian Dysfunction**
L1635: OD emerged as an AESI for nirogacestat based on observations made early in the conduct of Study NIR-DT-301. Evaluation of the data concerning this event, and review of the literature concerning the role of GS in ovarian function, support recognizing OD as an important identified risk for nirogacestat.
L1636: Note that OD was not available as a PT at the time of the amendment or in MedDRA v24.1, which is the MedDRA version used for this summary; therefore, for the purposes of identifying participants with this condition, OD was defined narrowly by the PTs of ovarian failure, premature menopause, amenorrhea, and menopause.
L1637: The majority of WOCBP receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID in Study NIR-DT-301 reported OD (27 of 36 WOCBP [75%]) as compared to 0 of 37 WOCBP receiving placebo. OD events resolved in all participants (n = 11; 100%) following discontinuation of nirogacestat with available follow-up data and also in the majority of participants (n = 9; 64%) remaining on study treatment with nirogacestat. OD events were accompanied by suppression of AMH and estradiol production, and increases in FSH, LH, and progesterone; however, nirogacestat treatment did not appear to result in a prolonged suppression of estradiol. Leading possibilities for the mechanism behind the observed ovarian events involve disruption of the growth and development of the ovarian follicle from the primary stage to the corpus luteum rather than damage or destruction of ovarian tissue or primordial follicles. The increased incidence observed in participants receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID compared to placebo, nature of the event, and its potential impact on the WOCBP experiencing it support OD being designated an important risk.
L1638: ## **Skin Events**
L1639: Skin events were an AESI in Study NIR-DT-301 based on observations with other GSIs (Takebe et al. 2014) and in early clinical studies of nirogacestat (Messersmith et al. 2015). Evaluation of the data concerning these events, and review of the literature concerning the role of GS in skin homeostasis, support recognizing skin rashes (rash maculopapular, dermatitis acneiform, rash, rash erythematous, rash pruritic, and rash papular), hidradenitis, folliculitis, and alopecia as identified risks for nirogacestat. In addition, based on the reports of Grade 3 events of maculopapular rash, hidradenitis, and folliculitis, these events have been designated as important risks.
L1640: Skin events were reported at a higher incidence in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID groups compared to the placebo group. Only 2 skin event reports were serious: 1 report of rash maculopapular from a participant in the nirogacestat 150 mg arm in Study NIR-DT-301 and
L1641: 1 report of rash in a participant transitioning from placebo to nirogacestat 150 mg BID in the OLE Pool.
L1642: Due to the increased incidence of skin events observed in participants receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID compared to placebo and the severity of the events in affected participants, hidradenitis suppurativa, folliculitis, and rash maculopapular are considered important identified risks.
L1643: ## **Electrolyte Insufficiency Events**
L1644: Hypophosphatemia and hypokalemia emerged as AESIs based on observations with other GSIs (Takebe et al. 2014) and in early clinical studies of nirogacestat (Messersmith et al. 2015). The increased incidence of hypophosphatemia and hypokalemia in the nirogacestat arm of Study NIR-DT-301 compared to the placebo arm, along with a plausible role for GS inhibition, the occurrence of Grade 3 events, and the frequent need for management with phosphate or potassium supplementation supports their recognition as important identified risks for nirogacestat.
L1645: The increased incidence of hypophosphatemia in the nirogacestat arm of Study NIR-DT-301 compared to the placebo arm, along with a plausible role for GS inhibition, supports its recognition as an identified risk of nirogacestat. The occurrence of Grade 3 events, and the frequent need for management with phosphate supplementation, support designating hypophosphatemia as an important event.
L1646: The possible mechanisms described for hypophosphatemia may also affect potassium homeostasis. The increased incidence of hypokalemia in the nirogacestat arm of Study NIR-DT-301 compared to the placebo arm and severity of the reported events, along with a plausible role for GS inhibition, supports recognizing hypokalemia as an important identified risk of nirogacestat.
L1647: ## **Elevated Liver Transaminases**
L1648: Effects on hepatic function were an AESI for nirogacestat based on observations of mild hepatic vacuolation in nonclinical studies in rats, and transient transaminase elevations in early clinical studies with nirogacestat.
L1649: The clinically relevant increased incidence of elevated transaminases in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID arm compared to the placebo arm in Study NIR-DT-301, and the time to first onset of the events during the first 3 cycles for most participants reporting an event, supports the identification of elevated transaminases as an identified risk associated with the use of nirogacestat. The occurrence of Grade 3 increases in transaminase values, along with a need to inform prescribers that some of these events may be managed with drug interruption followed
L1650: by dose reduction, rather than permanent drug discontinuation, supports designation of this risk as important.
L1651: ## Hypersensitivity Drug Reactions
L1652: A total of 5 participants receiving nirogacestat reported clinically relevant drug hypersensitivity / anaphylaxis events; however, only 2 events were suggestive of a hypersensitivity reaction to nirogacestat. The other 3 events were either associated with another concomitant medication (trimethoprim/sulfamethoxazole or morphine) or did not necessitate the immediate discontinuation of nirogacestat.
L1653: Overall, the infrequent occurrence of relevant events and the details of the individual events are not supportive of recognizing drug reactions (hypersensitivity and anaphylactic reaction) as risks for nirogacestat.
L1654: ## Applicant's Position
L1655: The data support recognizing OD, skin events, electrolyte insufficiency events, and elevated transaminases as important identified risks of nirogacestat. The recommended dose modifications for these events are provided in Table 58; diarrhea is not an AESI but it is an important identified risk described in Section 8.2.6. For other severe adverse reactions, or in the event of life-threatening adverse reactions, nirogacestat should be withheld until resolved to Grade 1 or baseline. Only restart at a dose of 100 mg BID after carefully considering the potential benefit and likelihood of recurrence of the adverse reaction. Nirogacestat should be permanently discontinued for recurrence of severe or life-threatening nirogacestat-related toxicity upon rechallenge at the reduced dose.
L1656: **Table 58:** Recommended Dose Modifications for Adverse Reactions
L1657: Adverse Reaction	Intervention
Diarrhea	
Grade 3 diarrhea persisting for \ge 3 days despite maximal medical therapy	Withhold drug until resolved to Grade \le 1 or baseline, then restart at a dose of 100 mg twice daily.
Electrolyte Abnormalities
L1658: Adverse Reaction	Intervention
Grade 3 hypophosphatemia persisting for \ge(b) days despite maximal replacement therapy	Withhold drug until resolved to Grade \le 1 or baseline, then restart at a dose of 100 mg twice daily
Grade 3 hypokalemia despite maximal replacement therapy	Withhold drug until resolved to Grade \le 1 or baseline, then restart at a dose of 100 mg twice daily
Elevated Liver Transaminases
ALT or AST \ge 3 to 5 times ULN	Withhold drug until ALT, AST or both are resolved to < 3 times ULN or baseline, then restart at a dose of 100 mg twice daily
ALT or AST > 5 times ULN	Permanently discontinue
L1659: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1660: **Ovarian toxicity:** Labeling contains an FDA-recommended Waring with respect to ovarian toxicity informing prescribers that female reproductive function and fertility may be impaired following treatment with nirogacestat. Labeling recommends monitoring patients for changes in menstrual cycle regularity or the development of symptoms of estrogen deficiency, including hot flashes, night sweats, and vaginal dryness. Overall 75% of women with reproductive potential (n=36) were reported to have an adverse event related to ovarian toxicity versus no patient in the control arm. Reported terms included ovarian failure, premature menopause, amenorrhea, and menopause. All events were Grade 2 in severity per the CTCAE definition. Per the ISS, 20 out of 27 patients reported resolution, including 11 that resolved off nirogacestat; however, per FDA analysis, the criteria to assess resolution were not adequate and therefore, additional study is needed.
L1661: Ultimately, because of potential overlap between terms such as ovarian failure, menopause, premature menopause, etc., a combined term was used in labeling. Additional sequelae of ovarian toxicity occurred during DeFi, such as hot flushes and weight gain (weight gain was similar to placebo in men but greater than placebo in women). These are not specifically described in labeling because they appeared to be downstream symptoms of ovarian toxicity (and there may have been variability with respect to reporting these symptoms versus a more global term such as premature menopause). With respect to hot flush events, 12 of 13 occurred in the setting of ovarian toxicity.
L1662: Due to limitations in the selected patient population, timing of reproductive hormone measurements, missing laboratory values, definitions of adverse events, and criteria to designate recovery, FDA was not able to clearly describe the incidence and severity of ovarian toxicity. Given these limitations, DUOG consultants (and DO3 agrees) recommended a Post Marketing Requirement to conduct a new study to characterize ovarian toxicity more adequately.
L1663: Consistent with reports of ovarian toxicity, changes in hormone levels were reported following treatment with nirogacestat including FSH, LH, AMH, and estrogen, and progesterone.
L1664: **Skin toxicity/events:** Based on the results observed in DeFi, cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma and basal cell carcinoma were diagnosed in 2.9% and 1.4% of patients, respectively. With respect to squamous cell carcinoma of the skin, one patient was diagnosed with a lesion on the left cheek and underwent Mohs surgery. The other patient's lesion occurred on the anterior thigh. FDA recommended inclusion of non-melanoma skin cancers in product labeling. As described above, GS may have a role in skin homeostasis. Rash, as a grouped (composite) term was identified in 68% of patients who received nirogacestat versus 14% in placebo with 6% of patients in the nirogacestat arm with Grade 3 rash. Dermatological events (terms) included rash maculo-papular, rash papular, rash, eczema, rash erythematous, dermatitis acneiform, palmar-plantar erythrodysaesthesia syndrome, dermatitis, and rash pruritic. The most common of these terms were rash maculopapular (32%), dermatitis acneiform (22%), and rash (19%). Alopecia and hidradenitis suppurativa were also reported. Dry skin and pruritis were reported; however, these were generally co-reported with other dermatological adverse events (consistent with a manifestation of the other rash-events). Specifically 10 of the 11 nirogacestat-treated patients with dry skin reported another skin event, compared to 1 of the 5 patients in the placebo arm.
L1665: Per the ISS, the median onset to first skin rash was 22 days with most patients having onset in the first cycle. Rash led to dose reduction in seven patients, dose interruption in 10 patients, and nirogacestat discontinuation in one patient. During DeFi, agents used to manage rash included topical steroids, antibiotics, and acne treatments.
L1666: Of note, FDA sent an information request to SpringWorks regarding bacterial infections given the potential signal noted during the assessment of all PTs and HLTS. The majority of bacterial infections in patients treated with nirogacestat in DeFi were folliculitis (9 of 14 events) which is described in labeling. Most other bacterial infections were also related to skin (e.g., paronychia, cellulitis, and staph abscess).
L1667: **Electrolyte insufficiency:** Electrolyte depletion, particularly for potassium and phosphorus can occur following treatment with nirogacestat. Labeling describes these risks in Section 5. In DeFi, these included decreased phosphate (65%) and decreased potassium (22%). Phosphate &lt;2
L1668: mg/dL occurred in 20% of patients who received OGSIVEO. Grade 3 decreased potassium occurred in 1.4% of patients. Final Labeling for nirogacestat recommends withholding nirogacestat (with future dose reduction) for Grade 3 or 4 hypophosphatemia persisting for 3 or more days despite maximal medical therapy and for Grade 3 or 4 hypokalemia despite maximal medical therapy.
L1669: There were no adverse event reports of magnesium; however, this electrolyte was not routinely collected during the conduct of the study.
L1670: With regard to hypophosphatemia, these events were frequently recurrent and six patients underwent dose modification (interruption, dose reduction, or discontinuation) for hypophosphatemia. Hypophosphatemia frequently occurred early with most patients being diagnosed with low phosphate during the first cycle.
L1671: With respect to potassium, per the CSR, the median onset to first hypokalemia event was 15 days in the nirogacestat arm. No patient was reported to have an SAE due to hypokalemia and there were no dose modifications due to hypokalemia.
L1672: **Elevated liver transaminases:** The FDA review team recommended inclusion of hepatotoxicity in the Warnings section of product labeling as ALT or AST elevations occurred in 30% and 33% of patients who received nirogacestat in DeFi, respectively. ALT or AST greater than 5 times the upper limit of normal occurred in 6% and 2.9% of patients, respectively. No report of Hy's law was observed during DeFi. Median onset to first increase AST or ALT was reported to be 22 days in the nirogacestat arm and the median duration of increased AST or ALT was 29 days.
L1673: In DeFi, nirogacestat led to dose reduction in one patient and drug discontinuation in three patients. In addition to the Warning, labeling also includes recommendations regarding increased AST or ALT including withholding nirogacestat for Grade 2 transaminitis (with a dose reduction).
L1674: **Hypersensitivity drug reactions:** In the application, SpringWorks identified 5 potential cases of clinically relevant drug hypersensitivity or anaphylaxis. At least two of the cases, upon review of narrative summaries in the SCS, appeared to be more likely related either trimethoprim / sulfamethoxazole or morphine. In the other three patients, the events appeared likely related to nirogacestat; however, based on the narratives, it was difficult to ascertain whether the events were hypersensitivity events or manifestations of other known nirogacestat-related adverse events, particularly rash and stomatitis.
L1675: ## 8.2.6. Other Noteworthy Adverse Events
L1676: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1677: Some of these events had also been identified as being of interest based on the available nirogacestat nonclinical data and information concerning other GSIs.
L1678: Clinically relevant increases in incidences in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID groups compared to the placebo arm in Study NIR-DT-301 were observed in the PTs of diarrhea, nausea, stomatitis, fatigue, epistaxis, upper respiratory tract infection, cough, dyspnea, and headache, along with the laboratory findings of Grade 1 proteinuria and glucosuria. Most of these events are not identified as important risks due to the absence or very low incidence of SAEs or Grade \ge 3 events for these risks; however, diarrhea is an important risk.
L1679: In Study NIR-301-DT, diarrhea occurred in 84% of participants who received nirogacestat compared to 35% in participants given placebo; Grade 3 diarrhea occurred in 16% of participants. Participants should be monitored and managed according to Table 58.
L1680: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1681: **Diarrhea** commonly occurred following treatment with nirogacestat with 84% of patients reporting diarrhea in DeFi versus 35% in the placebo arm (Grade 3: 16% versus 1.4%). Per the ISS, non-clinical data have suggested that GS inhibition “may interfere with the integrity of the lower gastrointestinal tract epithelium and control of intestinal motility.” In total, 129 diarrhea events were reported among the 58 nirogacestat-treated patients with diarrhea. Per the ISS, the median time to first reporting diarrhea was 9 days. Supportive medicines for diarrhea (e.g., loperamide) were used in 62% of patients with diarrhea in the nirogacestat arm.
L1682: **Stomatitis** in labeling consists of a composite term of different but related PTs. There was one event of an SAE due to stomatitis occurring on Day 9 where the patient developed Grade 3 stomatitis requiring treatment for infection and tramadol. The patient was later able to re-start treatment with a dose reduction.
L1683: **Dyspnea** occurred in 16% of patients in the nirogacestat arm versus 6% among patients who received placebo. Although the CSR assessed potential risk factors, e.g., based on location of disease, FAP status, or prior chemotherapy there were too few patients to provide any conclusions. Dyspnea could be long-lasting however, with the median duration of dyspnea reported as 197 days in the nirogacestat-treated arm.
L1684: ## 8.2.7. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1685: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1686: PROs are discussed in Section 8.1.3 and Section 8.1.9.
L1687: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1688: See FDA's assessments in Section 8.1.3 and Section 8.1.9.
L1689: ## 8.2.8. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1690: ## Data:
L1691: ## **Intrinsic factors**
L1692: The safety of nirogacestat was analyzed by the following subgroups: age, sex, race, ethnicity, BMI, other illness, and organ dysfunction. There were no overall differences in safety among participants based on age and the small numbers of non-White and Hispanic or Latino participants limits the ability to draw conclusions based on race or ethnicity.
L1693: As previously discussed, events associated with OD were reported at a higher incidence in female participants in the nirogacestat arm of Study NIR-DT-301 compared to the placebo arm (Section 8.2.5). Other TEAEs that occurred more frequently in females and in the nirogacestat arm include nausea, stomatitis, alopecia, influenza-like illness, hypokalemia, sinusitis, and dizziness. Hypophosphatemia was the only TEAE that occurred more frequently in males and had a higher incidence in the nirogacestat arm. These trends were also observed in the All DT and All CM Pools.
L1694: Apparent trends observed in the TEAEs by BMI subgroup analyses included: pruritis and dyspnea appeared to increase in incidence as BMI rose, at a level that was greater than that in the placebo group. Stomatitis and alanine aminotransferase increase occurred with greater incidence in the lower BMI subgroups, at a level that was greater than in the placebo group. However, no definitive conclusions could be made regarding the rates of TEAEs in select subgroups given the small number of participants within each subgroup.
L1695: ## **Extrinsic factors**
L1696: The safety of nirogacestat was analyzed by geographic location; no overall differences in safety among participants in these subgroups were observed.
L1697: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1698: There were no overall differences in safety among participants in most subgroups.
L1699: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1700: There were too few non-White or older patients to assess whether differences in safety exist in Study NIR-DT-301. Only 6 nirogacestat-treated patients were non-White and one nirogacestat-treated patient was Hispanic or Latino. Likewise, only 3 patients were 65 years of age or older (consistent with overall demographics of DT) in Study NIR-DT-301.
L1701: In the safety population, about two thirds of patients in both arms were women (44 of 69 for nirogacestat and 47 of 72 for placebo) with 25 men in both arms. Although the applicant conduct analyses of AEs by sex, such analyses should be considered exploratory and non-conclusive given the small number of patients. In general the AEs listed above in Data were those that occurred at a higher incidence rate in men or women. Per the CSR there were a limited additional terms (e.g., back pain in men or non-cardiac chest pain in women); however, the overall number of patients/events was low.
L1702: Most importantly, ovarian toxicity (see above) was limited to women enrolled in the trial. Alopecia occurred at a relative higher rate among women as compared to men (27% versus 4%).
L1703: ## 8.2.9. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1704: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1705: No studies have been conducted with nirogacestat specifically to address safety concerns.
L1706: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1707: FDA agrees; however, further assessment of ovarian toxicity will be requested in the post-marketing setting as a PMR (to further characterize the extent and reversibility of this finding).
L1708: ## 8.2.10. Additional Safety Explorations
L1709: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1710: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1711: No human carcinogenicity studies have been conducted with nirogacestat.
L1712: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1713: FDA agrees. Note that the non-clinical study was negative for carcinogenicity risk (refer to Section 5 above).
L1714: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1715: ## Data:
L1716: Studies with nirogacestat have not been conducted in women who are pregnant or lactating.
L1717: Reproductive toxicology studies in rats have been completed with nirogacestat and confirm the reproductive hazard of nirogacestat as a result of gamma-secretase inhibition in the developing fetus. In the fertility and early embryo toxicity studies (Studies 01214013 and 01214026), nirogacestat administration of doses of 20, 40, and 80 mg/kg/day prior to and during early pregnancy induced lower mating, fertility, and pregnancy indices compared to untreated rats and those dosed at 5 mg/kg/day. In pregnant rats treated with nirogacestat doses of 0, 5, 20, 50, or 150 mg/kg/day on gestation days 6 through 17, during the period of organogenesis, nirogacestat induced fetal loss and resorptions (Study 01214011). These fetal effects occurred at systemic exposures that are below those observed in participants at the proposed 150 mg dose level.
L1718: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1719: (b) (4) the patient should be apprised of the potential hazard to the fetus.
L1720: Since drugs are commonly excreted in human milk and because of the potential for serious adverse reactions in nursing infants, women should not breastfeed during treatment with nirogacestat and for at least 1 week after the last dose of nirogacestat.
L1721: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1722: Nirogacestat may cause ovarian toxicity in women of child bearing potential (see review above). A post marketing requirement to further assess reproductive function / ovarian toxicity is being requested to characterize the effects of nirogacestat more fully. Additionally, labeling will contained a warning regarding the risk of ovarian toxicity.
L1723: The non-clinical section of this review above describes findings from animal studies. In summary, oral administration of nirogacestat to pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis resulted in embryo-fetal toxicity and death at maternal exposures below the human exposure at the recommended dose of 150 mg twice daily.
L1724: Based on these findings, labeling will state that nirogacestat can cause fetal harm when administered to pregnant women. Labeling will advise women and men of reproductive potential to use effective contraception during treatment with OGSIVEO and for 1 week after the last dose. Labeling will also recommend against breast feeding during treatment with nirogacestat and for one week after the last dose.
L1725: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1726: ## Data:
L1727: No pediatric studies are included in this NDA submission; however, the open-label COG Study ARST1921 of nirogacestat in pediatric participants with DT is currently ongoing. A signal assessment was conducted following 2 AE reports (1 of epiphysiolytic and 1 of hip fracture) occurring in pediatric participants in Study ARST1921 and SpringWorks subsequently identified 2 additional relevant reports in 2 pediatric participants receiving nirogacestat via the compassionate use program for the treatment of DT (1 event of epiphyseal disorder and 1 event of osteonecrosis). At the time of the signal assessment analysis through the 13 December 2021 data cutoff, 26 pediatric participants have been exposed to nirogacestat in the compassionate use program (N = 8) and in the COG Study ARST1921 (N = 18). The total of 4 events occurring in 4 participants out of 26 participants is higher than would be expected in the healthy pediatric population (Peck et al. 2017; Palocaren 2018; Perry et al. 2018; Duffy et al. 2020).
L1728: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1729: The safety and effectiveness of nirogacestat have not been established in pediatric patients. Epiphyseal disorder, manifesting as a widening of the epiphyseal growth plate, has been reported in pediatric participants with open growth plates treated with nirogacestat. Increased physeal cartilage thickening and retention of the hypertrophic zone of the growth plate have been observed in rodents in animal studies with nirogacestat.
L1730: The available data support recognizing epiphyseal disorder as a potential risk for nirogacestat in pediatric participants with open growth plates, and the possible impact of this event on bone growth supports designating this potential risk as an important risk for pediatric participants.
L1731: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1732: The pediatric section of product labeling will contain a statement that the safety and effectiveness of nirogacestat have not been established in pediatric patients. Epiphyseal disorder, manifesting as a widening of the epiphyseal growth plate, has been reported in pediatric patients with open growth plates treated with nirogacestat.
L1733: The Children's Oncology Group (COG) is conducting study ARTST1921 to characterize the effects of nirogacestat in children with DT (&gt; 1 year of age). SpringWorks will submit the results of this study as a PMR with a projected study completion date milestone of September 2025. The results of this study may further inform labeling with respect to risk and benefit in younger patients with DT.
L1734: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1735: ## Data:
L1736: The NIR-DT-301 protocol defined overdose as any dose greater than 450 mg daily within a 24-hour period. The largest treatment dose of nirogacestat administered to oncology participants is 330 mg BID in Study A8641014 during the dose-finding stage of development, which 3 participants with solid tumors received. No Grade 4 or 5 TEAEs were reported by participants with solid tumors who received this dose. Dose-limiting toxicities were evaluable in 2 participants which included Grade 3 rash in 1 participant and Grade 1 palpitations and Grade 1 oropharyngeal pain in the other participant. TEAEs for all 3 participants with solid tumors who received this dose included the following PTs in more than one participant: diarrhea (2, Grade 1 and 2), rash (2, Grade 1 and 3), vomiting (2, both Grade 2).
L1737: Single doses of nirogacestat larger than these amounts should be managed by close observation and supportive measures as needed. Due to very high protein binding, dialysis is not anticipated to be effective for removing nirogacestat from the blood in participants with normal serum protein levels.
L1738: The potential for drug abuse, withdrawal, and rebound were assessed by reviewing reported TEAEs in the nervous system and psychiatric disorders SOCs for the presence of events that would suggest that nirogacestat has psychoactive properties, and for events that are included in the drug abuse and dependence SMQ. No TEAEs suggested nirogacestat could cause a psychoactive effect that could lead to drug abuse, physical dependence, or symptoms of withdrawal.
L1739: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1740: No potential for drug abuse, withdrawal, or rebound has been identified for nirogacestat.
L1741: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1742: FDA agrees.
L1743: ## 8.2.11. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1744: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1745: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1746: Not applicable.
L1747: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1748: FDA agrees as this is an NME.
L1749: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1750: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1751: Toxicities appear to have been adequately represented in the studies with nirogacestat. Potential safety concerns beyond the risks described in the proposed labeling are not expected. Enhanced pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for OD events and routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for unexpected adverse events.
L1752: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1753: In general FDA agrees; however, uncertainty exists with respect to long-term ovarian toxicity.
L1754: ## 8.2.12. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1755: ## Data
L1756: The most frequently (&gt; 30%) reported TEAEs in Study NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool were diarrhea, nausea, fatigue, hypophosphatemia, and rash maculopapular, which are expected with GS inhibition (Krop et al. 2012; Tolcher et al. 2012; Richter et al. 2014; Messersmith et al. 2015; Pant et al. 2016) and were generally aligned with the most commonly reported events in the All CM Pool and OLE Group.
L1757: Consistent with the NIR-DT-301 study, most participants in the All DT Pool reported their first TEAE during Cycle 1 (98% in the nirogacestat 150 mg BID group).
L1758: The incidences of TEAEs of Grade 3 occurring in the groups receiving nirogacestat 150 mg BID in the All DT, All CM Pool, and OLE Group were generally aligned with the population in Study NIR-DT-301, with diarrhea and hypophosphatemia being the most frequently reported Grade 3 TEAEs across all groups.
L1759: Nirogacestat was not considered as a cause of death for any reported event. The SAE data did not reveal any additional events with causality attributable to nirogacestat that were not already identified during the TEAE review. No new safety signals were observed in reviewing treatment-related SAEs.
L1760: Events leading to treatment discontinuation and occurring in &gt; 1 participant in the All DT Pool and All CM Pool were consistent with what was observed in Study NIR-DT-301. The most frequently (occurring in &gt; 1 participant in the nirogacestat 150 mg arm) reported PTs resulting in treatment discontinuation were diarrhea (4 participants), ALT increased (3 participants), premature menopause (3 participants), and AST increased (2 participants).
L1761: The following events are recognized as important identified risks for nirogacestat because of their increased incidence relative to placebo and also the potential impact to patients: OD, hidradenitis suppurative, folliculitis, rash maculopapular, hypophosphatemia, hypokalemia,
L1762: elevated transaminases, and diarrhea. With the exception of OD, these events have been observed in prior studies with GSIs and are expected events associated with gamma secretase inhibition (Krop et al. 2012; Tolcher et al. 2012; Richter et al. 2014; Messersmith et al. 2015; Pant et al. 2016). OD emerged as a new safety signal during the NIR-DT-301 study given the younger participation population included in studies of DT as compared to prior studies of other GSIs that were predominantly conducted in an older participant population for the treatment of Alzheimer's disease (Coric et al. 2012; Doody et al. 2013; Henley et al. 2014).
L1763: The following PTs were reported at an increased incidence relative to placebo and are recognized as identified risks, but not designated as important risks as these events were generally Grade 1 or 2 and did not result in discontinuation of study treatment: stomatitis, skin rashes (excluding rash maculopapular), alopecia, nausea, fatigue, epistaxis, URTI, cough, dyspnea, headache, proteinuria, and glucosuria.
L1764: The clinical data from the studies discussed in this NDA support epiphyseal disorder being recognized as a potential risk for nirogacestat in pediatric patients with open growth plates.
L1765: Nonclinical data support embryofetal toxicity being recognized as an important potential risk for nirogacestat [REDACTED] (b)(4). Impaired fertility in both male and female rats were also observed; however, effects on male and female fertility in humans are unknown.
L1766: The following relevant observations were made, but they do not represent adverse clinical findings: increased eosinophil values, increased basophil/leukocyte ratio, and increased eosinophil/leukocyte ratio.
L1767: No clinically significant changes in chemistry (other than hypophosphatemia, hypokalemia, and elevated transaminases), vital signs, ECG, or ECOG score data were observed.
L1768: The most clinically impactful identified risks associated with the use of nirogacestat 150 mg BID in the treatment of DT are OD, hidradenitis suppurative, folliculitis, rash maculopapular, hypophosphatemia, hypokalemia, elevated transaminases, and diarrhea. Except for OD and elevated liver transaminases, concomitant medications were frequently used to manage these events. Severe instances of these events were also managed with dose interruptions, followed by dose reductions in some instances. OD was observed to resolve both with continued dosing of nirogacestat and in all participants with drug discontinuation and did not appear to be associated with prolonged estrogen suppression. None of these events, nor any of the other risks associated with the use of nirogacestat, in isolation or in aggregate, would preclude the use of nirogacestat 150 mg BID for the treatment of patients with DT.
L1769: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1770: Overall, the safety population included in Study NIR-DT-301 and the All DT Pool is representative of the intended real-world population of patients with DT (Constantinidou 2012; Penel 2017; DTWG 2020; Anneburg 2022; Federman 2022). Therefore, the observed safety profile described in this SCS can be reasonably expected to reflect the safety profile when nirogacestat is used to treat adult patients with DT. No aspects of the observed safety profile would preclude the use of nirogacestat for treating adult patients with DT and the benefit:risk profile is favorable.
L1771: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1772: Treatment with nirogacestat resulted in frequent gastrointestinal toxicity, ovarian toxicity (in premenopausal women), dermatological toxicity, electrolyte wasting, and LFT abnormalities. In general, these adverse events were manageable with supportive care or dose modifications. Adverse events resulted in permanent discontinuation in 20% of patients, so a subset of patients may not be able to tolerate nirogacestat. Although there is uncertainty with respect to reversibility of ovarian toxicity (and a PMR will be requested), most adverse events appeared to be reversible following discontinuation. Furthermore, most adverse events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity; however, 16% of patients experienced Grade 3 diarrhea.
L1773: Although nirogacestat can cause moderate toxicity in some patients, DT is a rare disease without available systemic therapies. Approval of nirogacestat will allow for an additional treatment option in addition to local therapies, and prescribers (generally oncologists with expertise in sarcomas) can consider the optimal treatment for each patient (preferably in a multi-disciplinary setting). If not tolerated, most patients can discontinue without sequelae. Although nirogacestat can cause toxicity, patients also may benefit from treatment (e.g., due to pain reduction).
L1774: ## 8.3. SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1775: ## 8.3.1. Statistical Issues
L1776: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1777: The FDA evaluation of efficacy of nirogacestat was primarily based on the pivotal Study NIR-DT-301. Study NIR-DT-301 showed statistically significant improvements in PFS and ORR for nirogacestat compared to placebo. It should be noted that the primary PFS endpoint included both clinical and radiographic progression as assessed by blinded independent review. A sensitivity analysis of PFS based only on radiographic progression was consistent with the primary analysis of PFS. Although the PRO endpoints showed statistical significance, the estimates may not be reliable due to the large amount of missing data at cycle 10 and
L1778: differential missingness between arms. However, additional analyses conducted at earlier timepoints using patient-reported pain were supportive of the primary PFS results. Overall, FDA concluded that a clinically meaningful benefit for nirogacestat was demonstrated.
L1779: ## 8.3.2. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1780: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1781: Based on the totality of evidence, the multidisciplinary review teams recommend granting regular approval of nirogacestat 150 mg BID for the treatment of adult patients with progressing desmoid tumors who require systemic treatment. FDA recommends approval of nirogacestat for patients for whom systemic therapy is recommended because consensus guidelines recommend initial active surveillance for patients who do not need immediate treatment (e.g., vital organ involvement, and intolerable symptoms) (DTWG 2020, NCCN 2.2023). For patients with DT who do need to initiate treatment, management is heterogeneous, but systemic therapy is sometimes preferred over local therapy, such as surgery or radiotherapy (DTWG 2020).
L1782: The Applicant submitted data from 3 trials, among which Study NIR-DT-301 was the pivotal, randomized, placebo-controlled trial, and the remaining two trials (Study A8641014, and Study 14-C-0007) provided supporting data.
L1783: Study NIR-DT-301 demonstrated that nirogacestat 150 mg BID provided clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in PFS, where the median was not reached (NR, 95% CI: NE, NE), compared to placebo 15.1 months (95% CI: 8.4, NE) in patients with progressive DT based on RECIST 1.1. The ORR was also statistically different between the two arms with 41% (95% CI: 30, 54) and 8% (95% CI: 3, 17) in the nirogacestat, and placebo arms, respectively. The responses were durable with a median duration that was NR in both arms, NR (95% CI: NE, NE) with nirogacestat, and NR (95% CI: 8.3, NE) with placebo. Patient-reported pain improvement in the nirogacestat arm in patients experiencing baseline pain \ge2 supports the PFS and ORR results. Because DT is rarely fatal and the safety data for nirogacestat indicated that it would not lead to safety-related deaths, OS was not formally tested.

L1793: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1795: ## 10 PEDIATRICS
L1796: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1797: Reference is made to the Agreed iPSP letter (25 April 2022). The iPSP outlines a request for a partial product specific waiver for the subset of the pediatric population from birth to less than 24 months of age. In addition, the iPSP includes a deferral request for the results of Study ARST1921 to be submitted post-approval.
L1798: Study ARST1921 is an ongoing Phase 2 study to evaluate the safety, PK, and efficacy of nirogacestat in children and adolescents with progressive, surgically unresectable DT. The ARST1921 study was activated in September 2020 and is expected to take approximately 3 years to accrue. Participants will be followed for 2 years to evaluate the 2-year PFS rate allowing for the primary analysis including PFS in approximately 2026.
L1799: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1800: FDA granted the partial waiver request of PREA requirements for pediatric patients with DT from birth to less than 24 months of age. FDA will grant a deferral of the PREA requirements for patients aged 2 to 17 years where SpringWorks will submit the results of ARST1921 in the post-approval setting as a PMR (see Section 13 below).
L1801: An initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) was agreed upon between FDA and SpringWorks on April 25, 2022, for a partial waiver of PREA requirements for patients 0-&lt;2 years of age and a deferral
L1802: in older pediatric patients awaiting the results of Children's Oncology Group (COG) Study ARST1921, which will be submitted post-approval. ARST1921 is an ongoing Phase 2 study evaluating the safety, pharmacokinetics, and efficacy of nirogacestat in pediatric patients &gt;1 to &lt;18 years-old with progressive, surgically unresectable DT. Study ARST1921 was activated in September 2020 and was expected to take approximately 3 years to accrue; per clinicaltrials.gov, updated on 11/22/2023, this study is active but no longer accruing patients. Patients are being followed for 2 years to evaluate outcomes including safety.
L1803: Although Study ARST1921 allowed for the enrollment of patients above one year of age (rather than 2 years as described in the Waiver of the PREA requirement for patients ages 0&lt;2), FDA agreed with the waiver for children under age 2 years given the following:
L1804: - Recent research and guidelines espousing a conservative strategy for desmoid-type fibromatosis in children (Orbach D et al. 2017, DTWG Guidelines 2020);
- COG study requirements for non-resectable tumors, progressing tumors, and previous systemic treatment;
- Observed risk of widening of growth plates, and theoretical risk on organ maturation (however, this risk would be solely based on non-clinical embryonic studies of the NOTCH pathway and not on data observed in patients), and
- Age distribution of 59 patients (over 63 months) in prior COG trial ARST0321 (Skapek SX, et al, 2013) with 78% of 59 patients being 10 years of age or older (additionally ARST0321 did not require previous systemic treatment).
L1805: Given the above factors, enrollment of a sufficient number of patients younger than 24 months of age would be highly impracticable for this rare disease.

L1818: ## 13 POSTMARKETING REQUIREMENTS AND COMMITMENT
L1819: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1820: As a Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) (21 U.S.C. 355c) PMR as described above in this review, SpringWorks will submit the results of the following study that will assess safety and anti-DT effects in the pediatric population:
L1821: 4550-1 - Complete clinical Study ARST1921, "A Safety, Pharmacokinetic and Efficacy Study of a gamma Secretase Inhibitor, Nirogacestat (PF-03084014), in Children and Adolescents with Progressive, Surgically Unresectable Desmoid Tumors" in pediatric patients to further characterize the safety, pharmacokinetics, and anti-tumor activity of nirogacestat in pediatric patients 2 to 17 years with desmoid tumors. The study will include long-term monitoring to characterize epiphyseal disorders and ovarian toxicity in pediatric patients.
L1822: Study Completion: 09/2025
Final Report Submission: 09/2026
L1823: As discussed above and in the consult from DUOG, FDA is requiring SpringWorks, under Section 505(o)(3) of the FDCA to conduct a clinical trial to further assess the known serious risk of ovarian toxicity. As stated in the consult from DUOG, there were limitations of the DeFi design in the selected patient population, timing of reproductive hormone measurements, missing laboratory values, definitions of adverse events, and criteria to designate recovery. Given these limitations, a clinical trial was needed to ensure proper selection of patients, documentation of adverse events, assessment of labs (including timing), and recovery. SpringWorks will conduct the following trial:
L1824: 4550-2 - Conduct a prospective, open-label, single-arm clinical trial in adult premenopausal females with desmoid tumor to further characterize the incidence and severity of ovarian toxicity after a minimum of 12 months of nirogacestat treatment. Ensure that ovarian safety assessments after 12 months of continuous treatment are available from a minimum of 40 subjects. Post-treatment follow-up assessments including reproductive hormone measurements should be obtained at regularly scheduled intervals and after treatment cessation to assess time to ovarian function recovery.
L1825: Draft Protocol Submission:	07/2024
Final Protocol Submission:	10/2024
Trial Completion:	12/2028
Final Report Submission:	06/2029
L1826: Additionally, as a PMC, SpringWorks has agreed to assess the effects of proton pump inhibitors on the bioavailability of nirogacestat. SpringWorks may conduct a PBPK modeling study; however, if needed, depending on the results of the study, SpringWorks will conduct a clinical PK trial to assess the effects of proton pump inhibition on the PK of nirogacestat. The following in the language regarding the PMC:
L1827: 4550-3 - Conduct physiologic-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling study and subsequently a clinical pharmacokinetic trial if needed, to evaluate the effect of proton pump inhibitors (PPIs) on the bioavailability of nirogacestat. Design and conduct the trials and modeling analyses in accordance with the FDA guidance for industry, "Evaluation of Gastric pH-Dependent Drug Interactions with Acid-Reducing Agents: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Clinical Implications" and "Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Analyses — Format and Content Guidance for Industry."
L1828: Interim Report Submission (Final PBPK Analysis):	06/2024
Draft Protocol Submission:	01/2025
L1829: Final Protocol Submission: 04/2025
Trial Completion: 02/2026
Final Report Submission: 08/2026

L1884: ## 19.2. Financial Disclosure
L1885: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1886: See Sections 8.1.1 through Section 8.1.7 for financial disclosure information.
L1887: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1888: The Applicant submitted a Financial Interests and Arrangements of Clinical Investigators Certification for Study NIR-DT-301, which stated that the Applicant did not enter into any financial arrangements with the clinical investigators. However, FDA inspection revealed that four investigators had financial arrangements, none of which were disclosed by the Applicant. The FDA clinical reviewer requested the Applicant to provide details regarding these financial arrangements. After reviewing the Applicant's response, FDA concluded that the financial disclosure forms submitted by the Applicant were accurate, and that no investigators had financial interests or arrangements that were disclosable as defined in 21CFR54.2. See Section 4.1 for more details.

L1896: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1897: ## 19.4.1. Bioanalytical Review
L1898: ## 19.4.2. OSIS Executive Summary
L1899: There are thirteen clinical studies submitted to support this NDA, in which eleven clinical studies include pharmacokinetic (PK) data/information. OSIS reviewed the following method validation and bioanalytical reports which support eleven of the clinical studies. This review focuses on the data quality and reliability of (a) four method validations for the quantification of nirogacestat in human serum and human urine, (b) one method validation for the
L1900: quantification of [^{14}\text{C}]-nirogacestat in human serum, and (c) fifteen bioanalytical reports associated with eleven clinical studies.
L1901: OSIS finds (1) the results of 32 samples from bioanalytical study A2007079.01 are unreliable due to analysis outside of the established long-term stability window and (2) limited details were provided in the method validation 193/006V to support the quantification of ^{14}\text{C}-nirogacestat in human serum. These concerns related to bioanalytical report A2007079.01 (for clinical study NIR-DT-301) and method validation 193/006V (for clinical study NIR-DT-102) have been communicated to the Office of Clinical Pharmacology (OCP) review team.

L1918: ## (2) Method Validation Report AR0308A

Since the method cross-validation between the two (b) (4) locations was conducted and appears to be appropriate (report A864901), the method's inter-site reliability is considered established and acceptable.
